(4.10)

\displaystyle\tau_{q}\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\ell_{q}^{4\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\ell_{q}^{4\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\ell_{q}^{4\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\ell_{q}^{4\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}$}}$}}.

(4.11)

Remark 4.1
(name=Homogeneity in
\Lambdaup
of
\ell_{q},\tau_{q}
,label=thm:oss:EllqTgqLg).

\ell_{q}
, as well as the
\ell_{q,i}
defined in Section
8
, are 0-homogeneous in
\Lambdaup
, whereas
\tau_{q}
is
{}^{1\!}/_{2}
-homogeneous. The last property allows us to cancel the
\Lambdaup^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
factors we will see appearing in the course of the proof.

We also assume

\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda_{q+1}^{1-15\alpha-\beta\gamma}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda_{q+1}^{1-15\alpha-\beta\gamma}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda_{q+1}^{1-15\alpha-\beta\gamma}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda_{q+1}^{1-15\alpha-\beta\gamma}$}}$}}\leq\delta_{q+2},

which can be achieved if
a
is sufficiently large assuming
(15\alpha+\beta\gamma)b<(b-1)(1-\beta-2b\beta)
. Moreover, we assume

\displaystyle\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\leq{}

\displaystyle\ell_{q}\leq\lambda_{q}^{-1}.

(4.13)

The right inequality in (
4.13
) is evident from the definition. The left inequality can be reduced to
-b<\beta b^{2}(1+\gamma)+\beta-1-2b\alpha
, which can easily be verified for
\alpha=0=\gamma
, and thus also for
\alpha,\gamma
sufficiently small. We will in fact need the following sharper bound:

\displaystyle\lambda_{q+1}^{1-\overline{N}}\leq{}

\displaystyle\ell_{q}^{\overline{N}+1},

(4.14)

which can be achieved by imposing the following condition:

\overline{N}[(b-1)(1-\beta(b+1)\!)-\gamma\beta b^{2}-2\alpha b]>1+b+(1+\gamma)\beta b^{2}+2\alpha b-\beta.

The above conditions can be obtained by choosing, in this order

•

b,\beta
as in (
•
‣
4
), so that in particular
\beta(1+b)<1
;

•

0<\alpha,\gamma
sufficiently small depending on
b,\beta
;

•

\overline{N}\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large depending on
b,\beta,\alpha,\gamma
so as to get (
4
).

One last notational remark:
A\lesssim B
(resp.
A\gtrsim B
) will mean
A\leq C(b,\beta,\alpha,\gamma,M)B
(resp.
A\geq C(b,\beta,\alpha,\gamma,M)B
), or
C(N,b,\beta,\alpha,\gamma,M)
if norms depending on
N
are involved (e.g.
\mathcal{C}^{N+1+\alpha}
-norms).
A\sim B
will mean
A\lesssim B
and
A\gtrsim B
. Note that
C
does not depend on
a\gg 1
.

5
Main iterative propositions

In this section, we state the main propositions which allow us to pass from one kind of subsolution to another one, which is closer to the notion of solution. Both the below statements use the parameters
\delta_{q},\lambda_{q},\Lambdaup
defined in the previous section. The combination of these propositions leads to our main theorem, as illustrated in Section
6
.

In the first proposition it is shown that a smooth strict subsolution can be approximated with an adapted subsolution.

Proposition 5.1
(name=From strict to adapted subsolutions,label=thm:propo:S2A).

Let
(\tilde{v},\tilde{p},\tilde{R})
be a smooth strict subsolution on
[0,T]
. Then, for any
\theta<\hat{\beta}<{}^{1}/_{3}
,
\nu>\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1-3\hat{\beta}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2\hat{\beta}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1-3\hat{\beta}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2\hat{\beta}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1-3\hat{\beta}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2\hat{\beta}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1-3\hat{\beta}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2\hat{\beta}$}}$}}
, and
\delta,\sigma>0
, there exist
\gamma,\Omegaup>0
and a
\mathcal{C}^{\hat{\beta}}
-adapted subsolution
(\hat{v},\hat{p},\hat{R})
with parameters
\gamma,\Omegaup,\nu
such that
\hat{\rho}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 5$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}\delta
and, for all
t\!\in\![0,T]

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\big({\left|\hat{v}\right|}^{2}+\operatorname{tr}\hat{R}\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux={}

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big({\left|\tilde{v}\right|}^{2}+\operatorname{tr}\tilde{R}\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux

(5.1)

\displaystyle{\left\|\tilde{v}-\hat{v}_{\mathcal{C}^{0}}\lesssim{}\right\|}

\displaystyle 1+\delta^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}

(5.2)

\displaystyle{\left\|\tilde{v}-\hat{v}\right\|}_{H^{-1}}<{}

\displaystyle\sigma

(5.3)

Moreover, if we define

\hat{\mathcal{T}}(t)\coloneq\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\Big({\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}\right|}^{2}-{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\hat{v}\right|}^{2}\Big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus,

we have the bound

{\left|\partial_{t}\hat{\mathcal{T}}\right|}\lesssim\sum_{q}\Lambdaup\lambda_{q}^{\theta+\epsilon-\beta}.

The
q=0
term of this sum is the largest, and is
\delta\lambda_{1}^{2\beta}\lambda_{0}^{\theta+\epsilon-\beta}
, which is
a
-increasing. Since we can see that
a\to\infty
for
\delta\to 0
, for any
\eta>0
, it can only be ensured that

{\left|\!\!\hat{\,\,\mathcal{T}}\right|}(t)\leq\eta\qquad t\!\in\![0,\hat{T}(\eta,\delta,a)],

where
\hat{T}(\eta,\delta,a)\sim\eta\delta^{-1}\lambda_{1}^{-2\beta}\lambda_{0}^{\beta-\theta-\epsilon}\to 0
if
a\to\infty
or
\eta\to 0
.

The proof will be given in Section
10
.

Next, we show that adapted subsolutions can be approximated by weak solutions with the same initial data.

Proposition 5.2
(name=From adapted subsolutions to weak solutions,label=thm:propo:A2S).

Let
\theta<\beta<\hat{\beta}<\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}
,
\gamma>0
, and
\nu>0
with

\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1-3\hat{\beta}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2\hat{\beta}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1-3\hat{\beta}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2\hat{\beta}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1-3\hat{\beta}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2\hat{\beta}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1-3\hat{\beta}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2\hat{\beta}$}}$}}<\nu<\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1-3\beta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2\beta$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1-3\beta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2\beta$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1-3\beta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2\beta$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1-3\beta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2\beta$}}$}}.

The following holds for all
\delta<1
.

If
(\hat{v},\hat{p},\hat{R})
is a
\mathcal{C}^{\hat{\beta}}
-adapted subsolution with parameters
\gamma,\Omegaup,\nu
and
\hat{\rho}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 5$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta
, then, for all
\sigma>0
, there exists a
\mathcal{C}^{\beta}
weak solution
v
of (
1
) with initial datum

v(\cdot,0)=\hat{v}(\cdot,0)

and such that, for all
t\!\in\![0,T]

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|v\right|}^{2}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux={}

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big({\left|\hat{v}\right|}^{2}+\operatorname{tr}\hat{R}\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux

(5.8)

\displaystyle{\left\|v-\hat{v}\right\|}_{\mathcal{C}^{0}}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\delta^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}

(5.9)

\displaystyle{\left\|v-\hat{v}\right\|}_{H^{-1}}<{}

\displaystyle\sigma

(5.10)

Moreover, if we define

{\mathcal{T}}(t)\coloneq\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\Big({\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\hat{v}\right|}^{2}-{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}v\right|}^{2}\Big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus,

once again we can see that

{\left|\partial_{t}{\mathcal{T}}\right|}\lesssim\sum\Lambdaup\lambda_{q}^{\theta+\epsilon-\beta},

so that, like in the previous proposition, for any
\eta>0
, it can be ensured that

{\left|{\mathcal{T}}(t)\right|}\leq\eta\qquad\forall t\!\in\![0,T(\eta,\delta,a)],

where
T(\eta,\delta,a)\sim\eta\delta^{-1}\lambda_{1}^{-2\beta}\lambda_{0}^{\beta-\theta-\epsilon}\to 0
if
\eta\to 0
or
a\to\infty
.

Finally, consider the family of strong subsolutions
(\hat{v},\hat{p},\hat{R}+{}^{e}/_{3}\operatorname{Id})
, where
e:[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}
satisfies the following conditions:

\displaystyle e(t)\leq{}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 5$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta-\hat{\rho}(t)

(5.13)

\displaystyle{\left|\partial_{t}e\right|}\leq{}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\delta_{0}}\lambda_{0}e.

(5.14)

\displaystyle e\geq{}

\displaystyle 0.

(5.15)

This family can be used to yield infinitely many distinct weak solutions with the same initial data as
(v,p)
.

The proof will be given in Section
11
.

(??)
allows us to prove the following wildness criterion.

Corollary 5.1
(name=Wildness criterion,label=thm:cor:WildnessCrit).

Let
\theta<\beta<\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}
and
(\hat{v},\hat{p},\hat{R})
be a
\mathcal{C}^{\beta}
-adapted subsolution such that
\rho\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 5$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta
for some small
\delta>0
and
\rho^{-1}|\partial_{t}\rho|\leq M\delta
for some suitably large
M>0
. Assume that the following admissibility condition is satisfied for all
t\!\in\![0,t_{a}]
for some sufficiently small
t_{a}
:

\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big({\left|\hat{v}\right|}^{2}(x,t)+\operatorname{tr}\hat{R}(x,t)\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux+\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\hat{v}\right|}^{2}(x,s)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big({\left|\hat{v}\right|}^{2}(x,0)+\operatorname{tr}\hat{R}(x,0)\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux,

with a strict inequality for at least some
t\!\in\![0,t_{a}]
. Then
\hat{v}(x,0)\!\in\!W_{\theta,\beta-\epsilon,t_{a}}
for any
\epsilon>0
.

The existence of infinitely many
\mathcal{C}^{\beta-\epsilon}
weak solutions with
\hat{v}(x,0)
as their initial datum is a consequence of
(??)
above. The admissibility of those solutions follows from (
5.1
) as shown in the next section, where it is also seen that the strictness of (
5.1
) for at least some
t
is vital to the admissibility of the solutions.

We shall henceforth adopt the following notational convention, already applied in the statements of the propositions:

•

(\tilde{v},\tilde{p},\tilde{R})
will always denote strict subsolutions;

•

In Sections
6
-
7
,
(\check{v},\check{p},\check{R})
will always denote strong subsolutions; in Sections
8
-
9
, all subsolutions will be strong, and in Sections
10
-
11
, the subscripts will mark strong subsolutions;

•

(\hat{v},\hat{p},\hat{R})
will always denote adapted subsolutions, and
\hat{\beta}
will be the Hölder regularity of adapted subsolutions;

•

(v,p)
will always denote (weak) solutions.

6
Proof of the existence theorem

We start by recalling the following classical result.

Theorem 6.1
(name=Existence of Leray solutions,label=thm:teor:ExistLeraySol).

For any
w\!\in\!L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
with
\operatorname{div}w=0
and every
\theta\!\in\!(0,1)
there is a weak solution
v\!\in\!L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+},L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})\!)\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+},H^{\theta}(\mathbb{T}^{3})\!)
of (
1
) such that
v(\cdot,0)=w
and

\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|v\right|}^{2}(x,t)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux+\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}v\right|}^{2}(x,s)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|w\right|}^{2}(x)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\qquad\forall t\geq 0.

In fact, the following form of energy inequality also holds:

\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|v\right|}^{2}(x,t)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux+\int\limits_{s\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}v\right|}^{2}(x,\tau)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mu\tau\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|v\right|}^{2}(x,s)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\qquad\text{a.e. }s,\forall t>s.

Recalling the
(??)
of subsolutions and strict subsolutions, one can prove the following existence result.

Lemma 6.1
(name=Existence of strict subsolutions,label=thm:lemma:ExistStrictSubsol).

Let
w\!\in\!L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
with
\operatorname{div}w=0
. For any
\delta>0
there exists a smooth strict subsolution
(\tilde{v},\tilde{p},\tilde{R})
defined on

such that

\displaystyle{\left\|\tilde{v}|_{t=0}-w\right\|}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}\leq{}

\displaystyle\delta,

(6.2)

and for all
t\!\in\![0,T]

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big({\left|\tilde{v}\right|}^{2}(x,t)+\operatorname{tr}\tilde{R}(x,t)\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux+\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}\right|}^{2}(x,s)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus\leq{}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\left|w\right|}^{2}(x)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux+\delta.

(6.3)

The proof is an adaptation of the one of
[

, Lemma 6.8, p. 38]
, and is reported in Appendix
A

The proof of the main result then follows the steps of
[

, taking care of the additional dissipation by suitably increasing the energy of the starting strict subsolution.

Proof.
(
(??)
)

We choose
\eta>0,\theta<\beta<\beta^{\prime},w\!\in\!L^{2}
with
\operatorname{div}w=0
. Using the above result, we obtain a smooth strict subsolution
(\tilde{v}^{\prime},\tilde{p}^{\prime},\tilde{R}^{\prime})
on
[0,T]

6.2
)-(

) hold for some
\delta>0
which we will fix later. We now note that adding a smoothly time-dependent non-negative multiple of the identity to
\tilde{R}^{\prime}
does not change the fact that
(\tilde{v}^{\prime},\tilde{p}^{\prime},\tilde{R}^{\prime})
is a smooth strict subsolution. We may thus substitute our strict subsolution with
(\tilde{v},\tilde{p},\tilde{R})\coloneq(\tilde{v}^{\prime},\tilde{p}^{\prime},\tilde{R}^{\prime}+2(3|\mathbb{T}^{3}|)^{-1}e_{K}(t)\operatorname{Id})
, where
K
is a constant to be specified later in this proof,

. Combining the choice of

with (

), we obtain the following relations for

:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big({\left|\tilde{v}\right|}^{2}(x,0)+\operatorname{tr}\tilde{R}(x,0)\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux={}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\left|w\right|}^{2}(x)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta

(6.4)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big({\left|\tilde{v}\right|}^{2}(x,t)+\operatorname{tr}\tilde{R}(x,t)\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux+\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}\right|}^{2}(x,s)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus\leq{}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|w\right|}^{2}(x)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta-Kt.

(6.5)

Indeed, passing from
(\tilde{v}^{\prime},\tilde{p}^{\prime},\tilde{R}^{\prime})
to

adds a term

to the left-hand side, since

be the initial datum of

, and note that

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt\operatorname{tr}\tilde{R}(x,0)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux={}

\displaystyle{\left\|w\right\|}_{L^{2}}^{2}-{\left\|\tilde{v}_{0}\right\|}_{L^{2}}^{2}+3\delta\leq{\left\|w-\tilde{v}_{0}\right\|}_{L^{2}}({\left\|w\right\|}_{L^{2}}+{\left\|\tilde{v}_{0}\right\|}_{L^{2}})+3\delta\leq{}

\displaystyle{}\leq{}

\displaystyle\delta(2{\left\|w\right\|}_{L^{2}}+\delta)+3\delta\leq C(w)\delta.

(6.6)

Using
(??)
and
(??)
, we can produce a
\mathcal{C}^{\hat{\beta}}
-adapted subsolution

\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt(|\tilde{v}|^{2}+\operatorname{tr}\tilde{R})(x,t)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux=\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt(|\hat{v}|^{2}+\operatorname{tr}\hat{R})(x,t)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux=\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|v(x,t)\right|}^{2}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux,

and thus

{\left\|v(t)\right\|}_{2}^{2}-{\left\|\tilde{v}(t)\right\|}_{2}^{2}=\int\operatorname{tr}\tilde{R}(x,t)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux.

Thus, we first choose

and obtain

, and finally we conclude that

{\left\|v_{0}-\tilde{v}_{0}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq\eta^{2}\implies{\left\|\tilde{v}_{0}-v_{0}\right\|}_{L^{2}}\leq\eta.

As for the admissibility condition, choosing

), we have that

\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|v(x,t)\right|}^{2}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux+\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}v\right|}^{2}(x,s)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\\
{}\overset{\text{\eqref{eq:ThmEnEq}}}{=}\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\Big({\left|\tilde{v}(x,t)\right|}^{2}+\operatorname{tr}\tilde{R}(x,t)\Big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux-(\hat{\mathcal{T}}+{\mathcal{T}})(t)+\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}\right|}^{2}(x,s)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\\
{}\overset{\text{\eqref{eq:ThmEI2}}}{\leq}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\left|w\right|}^{2}(x)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta-t\overset{\text{\eqref{eq:ThmEI1}}}{\leq}\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big({\left|\tilde{v}_{0}\right|}^{2}+\operatorname{tr}\tilde{R}(x,0)\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\overset{\text{\eqref{eq:ThmEnEq}}}{=}\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|v\right|}^{2}(x,0)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux,

where the second-last inequality is strict for all
t\neq 0
where (
6.5
) is valid. This yields the energy inequality for
t
sufficiently small. Since we can only estimate
|\partial_{t}{\mathcal{T}}+\partial_{t}\hat{\mathcal{T}}|
with a quantity which is potentially unbounded as
\delta\to 0
(as seen in (
5.1
) and (
5.2
)), and
C(w)\delta<\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\eta^{2}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\eta^{2}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\eta^{2}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\eta^{2}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}
implies
\delta\to 0
as
\eta\to 0
, we conclude that our time
T(\eta)
of guaranteed admissibility satisfies

\lim_{\eta\to 0}T(\eta)=0.

So far, we have only obtained one solution for each
\eta
. Suppose that, from
(\tilde{v},\tilde{p},\tilde{R})
, we produced the adapted subsolution
(\hat{v},\hat{p},\hat{R})
, and from there the solution
(v,p)
. As noted in
(??)
, considering

(\hat{v}^{\prime},\hat{p}^{\prime},\hat{R}^{\prime})\coloneq{\left(\hat{v},\hat{p},\hat{R}+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle e$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle e$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$e$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$e$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}\operatorname{Id}\right)}

with
e
satisfying a suitable set of conditions, we can obtain more weak solutions and ensure these solutions are admissible up to
T(\eta)
. The required conditions are listed below.

1.

The first condition ensures
\hat{R}^{\prime}(\cdot,0)\equiv 0
:

e(0)=0;

2.

The second one ensures
\operatorname{tr}(\hat{R}^{\prime})\geq 0
:

e(t)\geq 0;

it would be enough to require
\hat{\rho}^{\prime}\geq|e|
, but we exclude
e<0
for convenience (cfr. Step 3 of Section
11
);

3.

The third one ensures the admissibility of the new solutions:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}|\mathbb{T}^{3}|e(t)\leq{}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big({\left|\hat{v}(x,0)\right|}^{2}+\operatorname{tr}\hat{R}(x,0)-{\left|\hat{v}(x,t)\right|}^{2}-\operatorname{tr}\hat{R}(x,t)\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux

\displaystyle{}-\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\hat{v}(x,s)\right|}^{2}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus-\hat{\mathcal{T}}-Kt,

K
being the same constant used to find
(v,p)
; since the right-hand side of the above inequality is strictly positive for all
t\neq 0
where
(v,p)
is admissible, this condition is compatible with requiring that
e\geq 0
as done above;

4.

The last conditions are (
5.13
)-(
5.15
).

This completes the proof.

\Diamondx

7
From strict to strong subsolutions

We state here an analogue of
[
14 (D-Sz)
, Proposition 3.1]
.

Proposition 7.1
(name=,label=thm:propo:S2S).

Let
(\tilde{v},\tilde{p},\tilde{R})
be a smooth solution of (
3.1
), and
S\!\in\!\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T];\mathcal{S}^{3\times 3}_{+})
be a smooth positive-definite matrix field. Fix
\overline{\alpha}\!\in\!(0,1)
and
\epsilon>0
. Then for any
\lambda>1
there exists a smooth solution
(\check{v},\check{p},\check{R})
of (
3.1
) with

\displaystyle(\check{v},\check{p},\check{R})={}

\displaystyle(\tilde{v},\tilde{p},\tilde{R})\quad\text{for }t\!\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.33334pt\kern-4.99998pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\in}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.33334pt\kern-4.99998pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\in}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.69444pt\kern-4.18054pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\in}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.36115pt\kern-3.68057pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\in}}}\!\operatorname{supp}\operatorname{tr}S,

(7.1)

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big(|\check{v}|^{2}+\operatorname{tr}\check{R}\big)}(x,t)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux={}

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big(|\tilde{v}|^{2}+\operatorname{tr}\tilde{R}\big)}(x,t)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\qquad\forall t\!\in\![0,T],

(7.2)

and the following estimates hold

\displaystyle{\left\|\check{v}-\tilde{v}\right\|}_{H^{-1}}\leq{}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle C$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle C$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$C$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$C$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda$}}$}}

(7.3)

\displaystyle{\left\|\check{v}\right\|}_{k}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\lambda^{k}\qquad k=1,2

(7.4)

\displaystyle{\left\|\tilde{R}-\check{R}-S\right\|}_{N}\leq{}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle C$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda^{1-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}-N}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle C$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda^{1-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}-N}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$C$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda^{1-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}-N}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$C$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda^{1-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}-N}$}}$}}

(7.5)

Moreover,
\operatorname{tr}(\tilde{R}(x,t)-\check{R}(x,t)-S(x,t)\!)\eqcolon\mu(t)
is a function of
t
only and satisfies

{\left|\mu^{\prime}\right|}(t)\leq C\lambda^{\overline{\alpha}}.

The constant
C\geq 1
above depends on
(v,p,R),S
and
\overline{\alpha}
, but not on
\lambda
. Finally, defining

\check{\mathcal{T}}(t)\coloneq\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\Big({\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\check{v}(x,s)\right|}^{2}-{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}(x,s)\right|}^{2}\Big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus,

we have that

{\left|\partial_{t}\check{\mathcal{T}}(t)\right|}\leq C\lambda^{2(\theta+\epsilon)}.

Proof.

Define the inverse flow of
\tilde{v}
,
\Phiup:\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{T}^{3}
, as the solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\partial_{t}\Phiup(x,t)+(\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla)\Phiup(x,t)=0\\
\Phiup(x,0)=x\qquad x\!\in\!\mathbb{T}^{3}\end{array}\right.,

and set

\overline{R}(x,t)=\mathfrak{D}\Phiup(x,t)S(x,t)\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup(x,t).

Observe that
\overline{R}
is defined on the compact set
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T]
and, being continuous, has a compact image
\mathcal{N}_{0}\coloneq\overline{R}(\mathbb{T}^{3}\times[0,T])\!\subset\!\mathcal{S}_{+}^{3\times 3}
.

By
(??)
there exists a smooth vector field
W:\mathcal{N}_{0}\times\mathbb{T}^{3}\to\mathbb{T}^{3}
satisfying the differential equations (
2.2
) and the integral equations (
2.2
). Define

\displaystyle w_{o}(x,t)={}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}\Phiup^{-1}W(\overline{R},\lambda\Phiup(x,t)\!)

\displaystyle w_{c}(x,t)={}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda$}}$}}\operatorname{curl}(\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup U(\overline{R},\lambda\Phiup(x,t)\!)\!)-w_{o},

where
U=U(R,\xi)
is defined as in (
2
) and thus satisfies
\operatorname{curl}_{\xi}U=W
. Moreover, set

\check{v}\coloneq\tilde{v}+w_{o}+w_{c}\qquad\check{p}=\tilde{p}+\overline{p}\qquad\check{R}\coloneq\tilde{R}-S-\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}-\mathcal{E}^{(2)},

where

\displaystyle\overline{p}\coloneq{}

\displaystyle-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}(w_{c}\cdot\check{v}+w_{o}\cdot w_{c})

\displaystyle\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(F)+(w_{c}\otimes\check{v}+w_{o}\otimes w_{c}+\overline{p}\operatorname{Id})

\displaystyle F\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}(w_{o}\otimes w_{o}-S)+(\partial_{t}+\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla)w_{o}

\displaystyle{}+[(w_{o}+w_{c})\cdot\nabla]\tilde{v}+\partial_{t}w_{c}+(-\Deltaup)^{\theta}(w_{o}+w_{c})

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(2)}\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}\cdot\!\mathop{}\mkern-2.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.56596pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.27084pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-2.0mu\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}{\Big({\left|\check{v}\right|}^{2}-{\left|\tilde{v}\right|}^{2}-\operatorname{tr}S\Big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\cdot\operatorname{Id},

with
\mathcal{R}
defined as in (
2.1
). By construction, the relation (
7.2
) holds,
\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}
is traceless,
\mathcal{E}^{(2)}
is only
t
-dependent, and
(\check{v},\check{p},\check{R})
solves (
3.1
). To verify this last claim, we can see that

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}(\check{v}\otimes\check{v}-\tilde{v}\otimes\tilde{v}-S+\overline{p}\operatorname{Id})+\partial_{t}(\check{v}-\tilde{v})+(-\Deltaup)^{\theta}(w_{o}+w_{c})

\displaystyle{}={}

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\check{v}+\operatorname{div}(\check{v}\otimes\check{v}-S+\mathaccent 23{R})+\nabla\check{p}+(-\Deltaup)^{\theta}\check{v}.

We call
w\coloneq w_{o}+w_{c}=\check{v}-\tilde{v}
. Recall that

\displaystyle W(R,\xi)={}

\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq 0}a_{k}(R)A_{k}e^{ik\cdot\xi}

\displaystyle U(R,\xi)={}

\displaystyle\sum_{k}a_{k}(R)\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle ik\times A_{k}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle|k|^{2}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle ik\times A_{k}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle|k|^{2}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$ik\times A_{k}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k|^{2}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$ik\times A_{k}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k|^{2}$}}$}}e^{ik\cdot\xi},

which are respectively (
2.8
) and (
2
), with the
a_{k}
satisfying (
2.10
). This allows us to decompose

\displaystyle w_{o}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq 0}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup^{-1}a_{k}(\mathfrak{D}\Phiup S\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup)A_{k}e^{ik\cdot\lambda\Phiup}=\sum_{k\neq 0}b_{k}e^{ik\cdot\lambda\Phiup}

(7.8)

\displaystyle w_{c}={}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle i$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle i$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$i$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$i$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda$}}$}}\sum_{k\neq 0}\nabla(a_{k}(\mathfrak{D}\Phiup S\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup)\!)\times\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup(k\times A_{k})$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle|k|^{2}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup(k\times A_{k})$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle|k|^{2}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup(k\times A_{k})$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k|^{2}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup(k\times A_{k})$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k|^{2}$}}$}}e^{ik\cdot\lambda\Phiup}=\sum_{k\neq 0}\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle c_{k}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle c_{k}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$c_{k}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$c_{k}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda$}}$}}e^{ik\cdot\lambda\Phiup}.

(7.9)

The estimate (
7.5
) is deduced by combining arguments from
[
14 (D-Sz)
]
with estimates for
\mathcal{E}^{(2)}
and
\mathcal{R}((-\Deltaup)^{\theta}w)
.
\mathcal{E}^{(2)}
is estimated in a similar fashion to how we estimate
\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}
below. To estimate
\mathcal{R}((-\Deltaup)^{\theta}w)
, using the fact that
[\mathcal{R},(-\Deltaup)^{\theta}]=0
and (
2.1
), we see that

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathcal{R}((-\Deltaup)^{\theta}w)\right\|}_{0}\lesssim{\left\|\mathcal{R}w\right\|}_{2\theta+\overline{\alpha}}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\bigg(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\left\|b_{k}+\lambda^{-1}c_{k}\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle|\lambda k|^{1-\overline{\alpha}-2\theta}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle{\left\|b_{k}+\lambda^{-1}c_{k}\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle|\lambda k|^{1-\overline{\alpha}-2\theta}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|b_{k}+\lambda^{-1}c_{k}\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|\lambda k|^{1-\overline{\alpha}-2\theta}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|b_{k}+\lambda^{-1}c_{k}\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|\lambda k|^{1-\overline{\alpha}-2\theta}$}}$}}+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\left\|b_{k}+\lambda^{-1}c_{k}\right\|}_{N+2\theta+\overline{\alpha}}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle|\lambda k|^{N-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle{\left\|b_{k}+\lambda^{-1}c_{k}\right\|}_{N+2\theta+\overline{\alpha}}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle|\lambda k|^{N-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|b_{k}+\lambda^{-1}c_{k}\right\|}_{N+2\theta+\overline{\alpha}}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|\lambda k|^{N-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|b_{k}+\lambda^{-1}c_{k}\right\|}_{N+2\theta+\overline{\alpha}}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|\lambda k|^{N-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}}$}}$}}

\displaystyle{\hskip 14.22636pt}+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\left\|b_{k}+\lambda^{-1}c_{k}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\Phiup\right\|}_{N+2\theta+\overline{\alpha}}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle|\lambda k|^{N-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle{\left\|b_{k}+\lambda^{-1}c_{k}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\Phiup\right\|}_{N+2\theta+\overline{\alpha}}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle|\lambda k|^{N-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|b_{k}+\lambda^{-1}c_{k}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\Phiup\right\|}_{N+2\theta+\overline{\alpha}}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|\lambda k|^{N-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|b_{k}+\lambda^{-1}c_{k}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\Phiup\right\|}_{N+2\theta+\overline{\alpha}}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|\lambda k|^{N-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}}$}}$}}\bigg)

\displaystyle{}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\lambda^{\overline{\alpha}+2\theta-1}\cdot\!\sum_{k}\bigg(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle|k|^{7-\overline{\alpha}-2\theta}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle|k|^{7-\overline{\alpha}-2\theta}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k|^{7-\overline{\alpha}-2\theta}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k|^{7-\overline{\alpha}-2\theta}$}}$}}+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1+C_{\Phiup}(N,\alpha,\theta)$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda^{N-1}|k|^{N+6-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1+C_{\Phiup}(N,\alpha,\theta)$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda^{N-1}|k|^{N+6-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1+C_{\Phiup}(N,\alpha,\theta)$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda^{N-1}|k|^{N+6-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1+C_{\Phiup}(N,\alpha,\theta)$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda^{N-1}|k|^{N+6-2\theta-\overline{\alpha}}$}}$}}\bigg),

where we used (
2.10
) to get the extra
|k|^{-6}
in each term, and the boundedness of
\Phiup
to get the
C_{\Phiup}(N,\alpha,\theta)
.

Concerning (
7.4
), the smoothness of
\Phiup,S
combined with (
2.10
) gives us

\max\{\|c_{k}\|_{N},\|b_{k}\|_{N}\}\lesssim|k|^{-m},

for all integers
m>0
, where the
b_{k}
and
c_{k}
are as in the decompositions of
w_{o},w_{c}
above. This easily allows us to conclude that

{\left\|w\right\|}_{N}\lesssim\lambda^{N},

since differentiating the exponential gives us a factor of
\lambda
for each derivative. We then note that

{\left\|\check{v}\right\|}_{N}\leq{\left\|\tilde{v}\right\|}_{N}+{\left\|w\right\|}_{N}\lesssim 1+\lambda^{N}\lesssim\lambda^{N},

where the second step used the smoothness of
\tilde{v}
. For
N=1,2
the above reduces to (
7.4
).

Estimate (
7.3
) is proved separately for
w_{c}
and
w_{o}
. The former is straightforward, since
w_{c}
is already of order
\lambda^{-1}
in
L^{2}\hookrightarrow H^{-1}
thanks to (
7.9
), and (
7.3
) is an
H^{-1}
estimate. For the latter, the main idea is to write

e^{i\lambda\Phiup\cdot k}=\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\partial_{j}e^{i\lambda\Phiup\cdot k}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle i\lambda\partial_{j}\Phiup\cdot k$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\partial_{j}e^{i\lambda\Phiup\cdot k}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle i\lambda\partial_{j}\Phiup\cdot k$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\partial_{j}e^{i\lambda\Phiup\cdot k}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$i\lambda\partial_{j}\Phiup\cdot k$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\partial_{j}e^{i\lambda\Phiup\cdot k}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$i\lambda\partial_{j}\Phiup\cdot k$}}$}},

and integrating by parts. One must then make sure that
j=j(x)
is chosen in such a way that the denominator is bounded from below. This can be done by using the fact that
\Phiup
is a diffeomorphism, which implies
|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup|
is bounded below.

To continue, we note that
\mu=\operatorname{tr}\mathcal{E}^{(2)}=\mathop{}\mkern-2.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.56596pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.27084pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-2.0mu\int|\check{v}|^{2}-|\tilde{v}|^{2}-\operatorname{tr}S\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux
.
\tilde{v}
and
\operatorname{tr}S
are both smooth, so they are bounded. In order to estimate
\int|\check{v}(t)|^{2}
, note that the following energy identity for
\check{v}
follows from (
3.1
):

\partial_{t}\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}|\check{v}|^{2}+\operatorname{div}{\left(\check{v}{\left(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle|\check{v}|^{2}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle|\check{v}|^{2}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$|\check{v}|^{2}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$|\check{v}|^{2}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}+\tilde{p}\right)}\mskip-4.0mu\right)}+\check{v}\cdot(-\Deltaup)^{\theta}\tilde{v}=-\check{v}\cdot\operatorname{div}(\mathaccent 23{\check{R}}-\mathcal{R}(\!(-\Deltaup)^{\theta}w)\!).

Moreover, with the arguments used to estimate
R_{11}
in
[
14 (D-Sz)
, pp. 18-20]
, we conclude that

{\left\|\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}-\mathcal{R}(\!(-\Deltaup)^{\theta}w)\right\|}_{0}\lesssim\lambda^{\overline{\alpha}-1}.

These two bounds, by integrating in
x
and using (
7.4
), yield

{\left|\dfrac{{\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mu}}{{\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mut}}\mathop{}\mkern-2.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.56596pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.27084pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-2.0mu\int\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}|\check{v}|^{2}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\right|}\lesssim\mathop{}\mkern-2.0mu\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\textstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\vbox{\hbox to5.27777pt{\hss$\scriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.56596pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\vbox{\hbox to4.27084pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle{-}$\hss}}$\hss}}\mkern-2.0mu\int|\check{v}|{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\theta}\tilde{v}\right|}+|\nabla\check{v}|{\left|\mathaccent 23{\check{R}}-\mathcal{R}(\!(-\Deltaup)^{\theta}w)\right|}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\leq C(1+\lambda^{\overline{\alpha}}).

Thus, the estimate (
7.1
) is proved.

The last thing left is to estimate
|\partial_{t}\check{\mathcal{T}}|
. Combining some simple calculations with (
7.4
), (
2.1
), (
2.1
), and (
2.2
), we obtain that

\displaystyle{\left|\partial_{t}\check{\mathcal{T}}\right|}={}

\displaystyle{\left|\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\check{v}\right|}^{2}-{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}\right|}^{2}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus\right|}={\left|\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt[2(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}+(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}w]\cdot(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}w\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus\right|}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle{\left|\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt2(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}\cdot(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}w\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus\right|}+[w]_{\theta+\epsilon}^{2}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus\lesssim\underbrace{{\left\|\tilde{v}\right\|}_{\theta+\epsilon}{\left\|w\right\|}_{\theta+\epsilon}}_{\eqcolon I}+\lambda^{2(\theta+\epsilon)}.

The velocity
\tilde{v}
is bounded by smoothness, so
I\leq K(\tilde{v},\theta,\epsilon)\lambda^{\theta+\epsilon}
. Since
\lambda>1
, this yields (
7.1
), thus concluding the proof.

\Diamondx

(??)
will be applied in the situation described by the following corollary.

Corollary 7.1
(name=Strict to strong,label=thm:cor:S2Suse1).

Let
(\tilde{v},\tilde{p},\tilde{R})
be a smooth strict subsolution on
[0,T]
. There exist
\tilde{\delta},\gamma>0
such that the following holds.

For any
0<\delta<\tilde{\delta}
,
\alpha,\gamma>0
and
0<\epsilon<\beta-\theta
sufficiently small, there exists a smooth strong subsolution
(\check{v},\check{p},\check{R})
with
\check{R}(x,t)=\check{\rho}(t)\operatorname{Id}+\mathaccent 23{\check{R}}(x,t)
, and a “dissipative trace term” as isolated in
(??)
, i.e.

\check{\mathcal{T}}(t)\coloneq\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\Big({\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}(x,s)\right|}^{2}-{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\check{v}(x,s)\right|}^{2}\Big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus,

such that, for all
t\!\in\![0,T]

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big(|\tilde{v}(x,t)|^{2}+\operatorname{tr}\tilde{R}(x,t)\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux={}

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big(|\check{v}(x,t)|^{2}+\operatorname{tr}\check{R}(x,t)\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux

(7.11)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}\delta\leq\check{\rho}\leq{}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 5$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}\delta

(7.12)

\displaystyle{\left|\mathaccent 23{\check{R}\,\,}\!\!\right|}\leq{}

\displaystyle\Lambdaup\check{\varrho}^{1+\gamma}

(7.13)

\displaystyle{\left\|\check{v}-\tilde{v}\right\|}_{H^{-1}}\leq{}

\displaystyle\delta\lambda_{0}^{-1}

(7.14)

\displaystyle{\left\|\check{v}\right\|}_{1+\alpha}\leq{}

\displaystyle\delta_{0}\lambda_{0}^{1+\alpha}

(7.15)

\displaystyle{\left|\partial_{t}\check{\rho}(t)\right|}\leq{}

\displaystyle\delta\delta_{0}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{0}

(7.16)

\displaystyle{\left|\partial_{t}\check{\mathcal{T}}(t)\right|}\leq{}

\displaystyle\Lambdaup^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{0}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{0}^{\theta+\epsilon}

(7.17)

\displaystyle{\left\|\check{v}\right\|}_{\theta+\epsilon}\leq{}

\displaystyle K(1+\delta_{0}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{0}^{\theta+\epsilon}).

(7.18)

where the constant
K
depends on
(\tilde{v},\tilde{p},\tilde{R})
and
\epsilon
, the parameters
\delta_{q},\lambda_{q},\zeta_{q},\Lambdaup
are defined as in (
4
) with sufficiently large
a
, and
\alpha
is the small parameter from Section
4
.

The proof will proceed by first reducing all the claims in the corollary to a series of conditions on
\lambda
, and then, at the end, proving that all those conditions can be satisfied simultaneously. This is necessary because some of them are upper bounds on
\lambda
, and some are lower bounds.

Proof.

Let

\tilde{\delta}\coloneq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\inf{\left\{\tilde{R}(x,t)\xi\cdot\xi:|\xi|=1,x\!\in\!\mathbb{T}^{3},t\!\in\![0,T]\right\}}.

Since
\tilde{R}
is a smooth positive definite tensor on a compact set,
\tilde{\delta}>0
. Then
S\coloneq\tilde{R}-\delta\operatorname{Id}
is positive definite for any
\delta<\tilde{\delta}
. We may in addition assume without loss of generality that
\delta\leq 1
. We apply
(??)
with
(\tilde{v},\tilde{p},\tilde{R}),S
, and
\overline{\alpha}\!\in\!(0,1),\epsilon>0
to be chosen below. This yields a smooth solution
(\check{v},\check{p},\check{R})
of (
3.1
) with properties (
7.2
), (
7.3
)-(
7.5
), and (
7.1
). We first note that (
7.11
) coincides with (
7.2
). Next, we observe that
\check{R}-\tilde{R}+S=\check{R}-\delta\operatorname{Id}
, so that, since
\mu(t)=\operatorname{tr}(\check{R}-\tilde{R}+S)
is a function of time only, the function

\check{\rho}=\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}\operatorname{tr}(\check{R}-\tilde{R}+S)+\delta

is independent of
x
.

Let us now prove (
7.12
). By the above and (
7.5
) for
N=0
, we have that

{\left|\check{\rho}-\delta\right|}=\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\left|\operatorname{tr}(\check{R}-\tilde{R}+S)\right|}\leq{\left\|\check{R}-\tilde{R}+S\right\|}_{0}\leq C\lambda^{2\theta+\overline{\alpha}-1},

We require now the following condition on
\lambda
:

C\lambda^{2\theta-1+\overline{\alpha}}\leq\delta_{0}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{0}^{2\theta-1+\overline{\alpha}}.

Then we notice that, for
\gamma
sufficiently small and
a
sufficiently large, we have that

Indeed, rewriting the above in terms of

and taking

{\left|\check{\rho}-\delta\right|}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}\delta_{1}\zeta_{1}=\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}\Lambdaup^{-\gamma}\delta^{1+\gamma}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}\delta,

The bound (

is smooth and thus bounded by a constant

, so that, by interpolation and (

Therefore, we will require

\displaystyle\lambda\geq{}

\displaystyle\overline{K}\delta_{0}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}(2\theta-1+\overline{\alpha})^{-1}}\lambda_{0}

(7.30)

\displaystyle\lambda\geq{}

(7.31)

(7.32)

\displaystyle\lambda\leq{}
