Finally, to estimate
IV
, we first note that

{\left\|h^{\prime}\right\|}_{N}={\left\|\sum_{j}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt2\eta_{j}\partial_{t}\eta_{j}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5muy\right\|}_{N}\lesssim\sum_{j}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt2{\left({\left\|\eta_{j}\right\|}_{N}{\left\|\partial_{t}\eta_{j}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|\eta_{j}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\partial_{t}\eta_{j}\right\|}_{N}\right)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5muy\leq K\tau_{q}^{-1},

where
K>0
is a constant. We used (
9.28
). The term
IV
is thus estimated by combining this bound with (
9.27
), (
9.8
), (
9.28
), and the fact
\psi\leq 1
:

{\left\|IV\right\|}_{N}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle K|\mathbb{T}^{3}|^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\rho_{i}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tau_{q}^{-1}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2c_{0}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle K|\mathbb{T}^{3}|^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\rho_{i}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tau_{q}^{-1}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2c_{0}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$K|\mathbb{T}^{3}|^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\rho_{i}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tau_{q}^{-1}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2c_{0}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$K|\mathbb{T}^{3}|^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\rho_{i}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tau_{q}^{-1}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2c_{0}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}$}}$}}{\left\|\eta_{i}\right\|}_{N}\lesssim\rho_{i}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tau_{q}^{-1}.

By combining the above bounds on
I,II,III,IV
, we conclude that

{\left\|D_{t}\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\right\|}_{N}\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tau_{q}^{-1}\ell_{q,i}^{-N}.

To prove (
9.43
) and (
9.46
), we note that

\displaystyle{\left\|b_{i,k}\right\|}_{N}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle{\left\|\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\right\|}_{N}{\left\|a_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|a_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{N}

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}b_{i,k}\right\|}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\right\|}_{N}{\left\|a_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|D_{t}\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\right\|}_{n}{\left\|a_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{N}+{\left\|\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\right\|}_{N}{\left\|D_{t}[a_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})]\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|D_{t}[a_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})]\right\|}_{N}.

The bounds (
9.43
) and (
9.46
) then readily follow by combining (
9
), (
9
), and the following applications of (
2.4
):

\displaystyle{\left\|a_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{N}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathfrak{D}a_{k}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{N-1}+{\left\|\mathfrak{D}a_{k}\right\|}_{N-1}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{0}^{N}\lesssim{\left\|a_{k}\right\|}_{N}({\left\|\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{N}+{\left\|\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{1}^{N}),

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}(a_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\!)\right\|}_{N}\leq{}

\displaystyle{\left\|(\mathfrak{D}_{2}a_{k})(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{N}{\left\|D_{t}\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|(\mathfrak{D}_{2}a_{k})(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{0}{\left\|D_{t}\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{N}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle({\left\|a_{k}\right\|}_{N+1}{\left\|\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{1}^{N}+{\left\|a_{k}\right\|}_{2}{\left\|\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{N}){\left\|D_{t}\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|a_{k}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|D_{t}\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{N},

where
(\mathfrak{D}_{2}a_{k})_{ij}=\partial_{S_{ij}}a_{k}
being the matrix of the first derivatives of
a_{k}
w.r.t. the components of its argument.

To prove (
9.44
), we note that, by Leibniz rule

{\left\|c_{i,k}\right\|}_{N}\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{N}{\left\|b_{i,k}\right\|}_{i+1}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{N-i},

from which (
9.44
) follows by (
9.43
) and (
9.40
). Coming finally to (
9.47
), we start by noting that

D_{t}\nabla(b_{i,k})=\nabla D_{t}(b_{i,k})+[v\cdot\nabla,\nabla](b_{i,k}).

This means that

{\left\|D_{t}c_{i,k}\right\|}_{N}\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{N}{\left({\left\|\nabla D_{t}(b_{i,k})\right\|}_{i}+{\left\|[v\cdot\nabla,\nabla](b_{i,k})\right\|}_{i}\right)}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{N-i}+\sum_{i=0}^{N}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{i}{\left\|D_{t}\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{N-i}.

Since, by the estimates (
9.41
), (
9.40
), (
9.43
), (
9.46
), and (
9.11
) on the factors here involved, we see that this scales like
\ell_{q,i}^{-N}
, we will only need to prove the case
N=0
. In that case, we obtain that

{\left\|D_{t}c_{i,k}\right\|}_{0}\lesssim{\left\|D_{t}(b_{i,k})\right\|}_{1}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|[v\cdot\nabla,\nabla](b_{i,k})\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|b_{i,k}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|D_{t}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{0}\eqcolon I+II+III.

By (
9.46
) and (
9.40
), we see that

I\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tau_{q}^{-1}\ell_{q,i}^{-1}|k|^{-6}.

By (
9.43
), (
9.41
), and (
9
), we estimate
III
as

III\lesssim\rho_{i}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}|k|^{-6}\ell_{q,i}^{-1}\cdot\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}^{1+\alpha}\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tau_{q}^{-1}\ell_{q,i}^{-1}|k|^{-6}.

We are then left with proving that
II
also satisfies this bound. To this end, we rewrite the commutator as

[v\cdot\nabla,\nabla](b_{i,k})=\sum_{j\ell}(v_{j}\partial_{j}(\partial_{\ell}b_{i,k})-\partial_{\ell}(v_{j}\partial_{j}b_{i,k})\!)e_{\ell}=-\sum_{j\ell}\partial_{\ell}v_{j}\partial_{j}b_{i,k}e_{\ell}=\nabla v\nabla b_{i,k}.

It then follows from (
9.40
), (
9.43
), and (
9
) that
II
satisfies the same bound as
I
and
III
, thus proving (
9.47
).

In turn, the estimates on
\tilde{v}
in (
9.17
)-(
9.18
) follow from the ones just given precisely as in
[
4 (B-DL-Sz-V)
, Corollary 5.8, pp. 23-24]
. Indeed, once we note that

{\left\|\nabla(e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}})\right\|}_{0}\leq\lambda_{q+1}|k|{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{0}\leq 2\lambda_{q+1}|k|,

we can deduce from the estimates above that

\displaystyle{\left\|w_{o,i}\right\|}_{N}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{N}{\left\|b_{i,k}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}\right\|}_{0}+\sum_{i,k}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|b_{i,k}\right\|}_{N}{\left\|e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}\right\|}_{0}

\displaystyle{}+\sum_{i,k}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|b_{i,k}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}\right\|}_{N}\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}.

The
w_{o,i}
have pairwise disjoint supports, so the sum over
i
always consists of a single term, which yields that the desired estimates hold for
w_{o}
. The estimates on
c_{i,k}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}
are always better than those on
b_{i,k}
, meaning that any estimate that holds for
w_{o}
holds for
w_{c}
as well. Thus, (
9.17
) follows directly, and (
9.18
) and (
9.19
) follow by interpolation.

Coming to (
9.16
), the fact that
w_{c}
satisfies this bound can be easily deduced from (
9.44
), which tells us that
\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\|c_{i,k}\|_{0}\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}|k|^{-6}\ell_{q,i}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}
. To estimate
w_{o}
, we use a procedure similar to the one employed in Section
7
to prove (
7.3
), replacing (
7
) with (
9.43
).

Step 4: Estimates on the new Reynolds term
\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}
.

The aim of this section is to prove
\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}
satisfies (
9.20
), namely

{\left\|\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}\right\|}_{0}\leq\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-6\alpha}.

Drawing from
[
4 (B-DL-Sz-V)
]
, we decompose
\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}
as

\displaystyle\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}={}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}{\bigg(\partial_{t}w+\operatorname{div}(v\otimes w+w\otimes v+w\otimes w)-\sum_{i}\nabla\sigma_{i}+(-\Deltaup)^{\theta}w-\operatorname{div}S\bigg)}

\displaystyle{}={}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}{\bigg(\partial_{t}w+w\cdot\nabla v+v\cdot\nabla w+\operatorname{div}(w\otimes w)-\sum_{i}\nabla\sigma_{i}+(-\Deltaup)^{\theta}w-\operatorname{div}S\bigg)}

\displaystyle{}={}

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathcal{R}(w\cdot\nabla v)}_{\mathclap{\text{Nash error}{}\eqcolon\mathcal{E}_{N}}}+\underbrace{\mathcal{R}(\!(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla)w)}_{\text{Transport error}{}\eqcolon\mathcal{E}_{T}}+\underbrace{\mathcal{R}{\bigg[\operatorname{div}(w\otimes w-\mathaccent 23{S})-\sum_{i}\nabla\sigma_{i}\bigg]}}_{\text{Oscillation error}{}\eqcolon\mathcal{E}_{O}}+\underbrace{\mathcal{R}(\!(-\Deltaup)^{\theta}w)}_{\mathclap{\text{Dissipation error}{}\eqcolon\mathcal{E}_{D}}}.

We then note that, since the
w_{o,i}
have disjoint supports and
w_{o}=\sum_{i}w_{o,i}
, by (
9
), we have that

\operatorname{div}(w\otimes w-\mathaccent 23{S})-\sum_{i}\nabla\sigma_{i}=\operatorname{div}(w_{o}\otimes w_{c}+w_{c}\otimes w_{o}+w_{c}\otimes w_{c})+\sum_{i}{\left[\operatorname{div}{\left(w_{o,i}\otimes w_{o,i}-\eta_{i}^{2}\mathaccent 23{S}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}\operatorname{tr}S_{i}\right)}\right]}.

We now rewrite the first three terms using the definition of
w
, (
9
) (to rewrite
\mathcal{E}_{O}
), and the fact that
D_{t}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}=0
(to rewrite
\mathcal{E}_{T}
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{N}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k}\mathcal{R}(\!(\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}A_{k}+\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}c_{i,k})\cdot\nabla ve^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}})

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{T}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k}\mathcal{R}(\!(D_{t}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}A_{k}+\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}b_{i,k}A_{k}+\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}D_{t}c_{i,k})e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}})

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{O}={}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\operatorname{div}(w_{o}\otimes w_{c}+w_{c}\otimes w_{o}+w_{c}\otimes w_{c})+\sum_{i}\mathcal{R}\operatorname{div}(w_{o,i}\otimes w_{o,i}-S_{i})

\displaystyle{}={}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\operatorname{div}(w_{o}\otimes w_{c}+w_{c}\otimes w_{o}+w_{c}\otimes w_{c})+\sum_{i,k}\mathcal{R}(\operatorname{div}(\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup_{i}^{-1})e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}),

where the
C_{k}
are as defined in
(??)
. We now note that the leading order terms are

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(L)}\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k}\mathcal{R}(\!(\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}A_{k})\cdot\nabla ve^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}})

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{T}^{(L)}\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k}\mathcal{R}(\!(D_{t}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}+\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}b_{i,k})A_{k}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}})

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{O}^{(L)}\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\operatorname{div}(w_{o}\otimes w_{c}+w_{c}\otimes w_{o})+\sum_{i,k}\mathcal{R}(\operatorname{div}(\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup_{i}^{-1})e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}})\eqcolon\mathcal{E}_{O}^{(L,1)}+\mathcal{E}_{O}^{(L,2)}.

We start by estimating
\mathcal{E}_{O}^{(L,1)}
. Since
\mathcal{R}\operatorname{div}
is Calderón-Zygmund, we have that

{\left\|\mathcal{R}\operatorname{div}(w_{o}\otimes w_{c}+w_{c}\otimes w_{o})\right\|}_{\alpha}\lesssim{\left\|w_{o}\right\|}_{\alpha}{\left\|w_{c}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|w_{o}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|w_{c}\right\|}_{\alpha}.

From (
9.43
), (
9.40
), and (
9.44
), we can conclude that

{\left\|w_{o}\right\|}_{N}\lesssim\rho_{i}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\qquad{\left\|w_{c}\right\|}_{N}\lesssim\rho_{i}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{N-1}\ell_{q,i}^{-1}.

By interpolation, this lets us conclude that

{\left\|\mathcal{E}_{O}^{(L,1)}\right\|}_{\alpha}\lesssim\rho_{i}\ell_{q,i}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha-1}.

To estimate the other leading terms, we start by using
(??)
on all three:

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(L)}\right\|}_{\alpha}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k}{\left(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\cdot\nabla v\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{1-\alpha}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\cdot\nabla v\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{1-\alpha}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\cdot\nabla v\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{1-\alpha}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\cdot\nabla v\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{1-\alpha}$}}$}}+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\cdot\nabla v\right\|}_{N+\alpha}+{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\cdot\nabla v\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{N-\alpha}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\cdot\nabla v\right\|}_{N+\alpha}+{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\cdot\nabla v\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{N-\alpha}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\cdot\nabla v\right\|}_{N+\alpha}+{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\cdot\nabla v\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{N-\alpha}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\cdot\nabla v\right\|}_{N+\alpha}+{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\cdot\nabla v\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{N-\alpha}$}}$}}\right)}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathcal{E}_{T}^{(L)}\right\|}_{\alpha}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k}\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}+\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{1-\alpha}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle{\left\|D_{t}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}+\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{1-\alpha}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|D_{t}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}+\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{1-\alpha}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|D_{t}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}+\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{1-\alpha}$}}$}}

\displaystyle{}+\sum_{i,k}\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}+\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}+{\left\|D_{t}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}+\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{N-\alpha}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle{\left\|D_{t}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}+\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}+{\left\|D_{t}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}+\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{N-\alpha}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|D_{t}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}+\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}+{\left\|D_{t}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}+\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{N-\alpha}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|D_{t}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}+\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}+{\left\|D_{t}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}+\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{N-\alpha}$}}$}}

To estimate

, we combine (

{}\lesssim\sum_{i,k}\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|b_{i,k}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\nabla Notice how the leading order term here is the one that does not depend on
N
thanks to the
\ell_{q}
in (
4.14
). This is also true of
\mathcal{E}_{T}^{(L)}
and
\mathcal{E}_{O}^{(L,2)}
.

\mathcal{E}_{T}^{(L)}
is estimated in a similar manner, using (
9.40
), (
9.41
), (
9.43
), (
9.46
), and (
4.14
).

As for
\mathcal{E}_{O}^{(L,1)}
, we first ensure that adding a derivative, whichever factor it lands on, costs at most
\ell_{q,i}^{-1}
. This ensures that the leading term is the first one, because of that gain of
\ell_{q}
mentioned above. We then estimate the leading term.

By (
9.40
) and (
9.43
), differentiating
b_{i,k}
or
\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}
costs
\ell_{q,i}^{-1}
, and by (
9.30
), differentiating
\sigma_{i}
does not cost anything, so we are left with showing that
C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})
scales like
\ell_{q,i}^{-N}
. Thanks to (
2.4
), (
2.11
), and (
9.42
), we have that

{\left\|C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{N}\lesssim{\left\|C_{k}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{N-1}+{\left\|\nabla C_{k}\right\|}_{N-1}{\left\|\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{1}^{N}\lesssim|k|^{-6}\ell_{q,i}^{-N}.

We then use (
9.7
), (
9.40
), and the above estimate on
C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})
to estimate the leading term:

\sum_{i,k}\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\left\|\operatorname{div}(\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup_{i}^{-1})\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\left|k\lambda_{q+1}\right|}^{1-\alpha}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle{\left\|\operatorname{div}(\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup_{i}^{-1})\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle{\left|k\lambda_{q+1}\right|}^{1-\alpha}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|\operatorname{div}(\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup_{i}^{-1})\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{${\left|k\lambda_{q+1}\right|}^{1-\alpha}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|\operatorname{div}(\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup_{i}^{-1})\right\|}_{0}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{${\left|k\lambda_{q+1}\right|}^{1-\alpha}$}}$}}\\
{}\lesssim\sum_{i,k}\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\left\|\sigma_{i}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{0}^{2}{\left\|C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{0}+2{\left\|\sigma_{i}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|\sigma_{i}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{0}^{2}{\left\|C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{1}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{1-\alpha}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle{\left\|\sigma_{i}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{0}^{2}{\left\|C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{0}+2{\left\|\sigma_{i}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|\sigma_{i}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{0}^{2}{\left\|C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{1}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{1-\alpha}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|\sigma_{i}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{0}^{2}{\left\|C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{0}+2{\left\|\sigma_{i}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|\sigma_{i}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{0}^{2}{\left\|C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{1}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{1-\alpha}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|\sigma_{i}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{0}^{2}{\left\|C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{0}+2{\left\|\sigma_{i}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}^{T}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|\sigma_{i}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\right\|}_{0}^{2}{\left\|C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{1}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k\lambda_{q+1}|^{1-\alpha}$}}$}}\\
{}\lesssim\rho_{i}\ell_{q,i}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}.

\displaystyle\max_{i}{\left\|\mathcal{R}\partial_{t}w_{o}\right\|}_{\alpha}\lesssim\sum_{i,k}{\left(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{1}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle|k|^{1-\alpha}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{1}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle|k|^{1-\alpha}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{1}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k|^{1-\alpha}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{1}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k|^{1-\alpha}$}}$}}+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{N+1+\alpha}+{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle|k|^{N-\alpha}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{N+1+\alpha}+{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle|k|^{N-\alpha}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{N+1+\alpha}+{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k|^{N-\alpha}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{${\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{N+1+\alpha}+{\left\|\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|\Phiup_{i}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k|^{N-\alpha}$}}$}}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}.

(9.64)

Such estimates analogously also hold for
\mathcal{R}w_{c}
, and thus for
\mathcal{R}w
. Thus, by (
9
)-(
9.64
)

{\left\|\mathcal{R}(-\Deltaup)^{\theta}w\right\|}_{0}\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}\lambda_{q+1}^{2\theta+\epsilon-1}.

In particular, for
a\gg 1
large enough, (
9.20
) is satisfied if

2\theta+\epsilon-1+\alpha-\beta<-2b\beta-6\alpha\iff 7\alpha+\epsilon<1+\beta-2\theta-2b\beta.

Since
\theta<\beta
, and
2b\beta<1-\beta
by (
•
‣
4
), we have that
1+\beta-2\theta-2b\beta>0
. Thus, (
9
) above holds for
\alpha,\epsilon
sufficiently small.

Step 5: Estimates on the new Reynolds term

Now we turn to

\displaystyle|k|^{-6}\delta_{q+1}\ell_{q}^{-N}.

(9.69)

Concerning the third term in (

\displaystyle{}+\sum_{i}\sum_{k\neq 0}{\left|\int(\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i})^{-1}b_{i,k}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}c_{i,-k}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\right|}\eqcolon I+II,

where we used

in the case

{\left|\mathcal{E}\right|}^{(2)}\lesssim\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta_{q+1}\ell_{q}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda_{q+1}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta_{q+1}\ell_{q}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda_{q+1}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta_{q+1}\ell_{q}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda_{q+1}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta_{q+1}\ell_{q}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda_{q+1}$}}$}}+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}^{1+\alpha}\ell_{q}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda_{q+1}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}^{1+\alpha}\ell_{q}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda_{q+1}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}^{1+\alpha}\ell_{q}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda_{q+1}$}}$}}+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\rho_{i}\ell_{q,i}^{-1}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\lambda_{q+1}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\rho_{i}\ell_{q,i}^{-1}$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\lambda_{q+1}$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\rho_{i}\ell_{q,i}^{-1}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda_{q+1}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\rho_{i}\ell_{q,i}^{-1}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\lambda_{q+1}$}}$}}.

By (
4
), we thus conclude that, for
a\gg 1
sufficiently large,
\mathcal{E}^{(2)}
satisfies (
9.20
). Combining with the fact (obtained in the previous step) that
\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}
satisfies (
9.20
), we thus conclude that (
9.20
) holds.

Step 6: Estimates on
\partial_{t}\operatorname{tr}\mathcal{E}

Observe that
\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}^{(1)}
is traceless, whereas
\mathcal{E}^{(2)}
is a function of
t
only. In order to estimate the time derivative of
\mathcal{E}^{(2)}
, observe that, since
v
is solenoidal, for every
F=F(x,t)

\dfrac{{\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mu}}{{\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mut}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0ptF=\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0ptD_{t}F,

where
D_{t}=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla
. Therefore, using again the decomposition in (
9.66
), we have that

\displaystyle{\left|\dfrac{{\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mu}}{{\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mut}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt|\tilde{v}|^{2}-|v|^{2}-\operatorname{tr}S\right|}\leq{}

\displaystyle{\left|\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt\operatorname{tr}{\left[D_{t}{\left(\sum_{i,k\neq 0}\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-T}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}\right)}\right]}\right|}

\displaystyle{}+{\left|\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0ptD_{t}(2w_{c}\cdot w_{o}+|w_{c}|^{2})\right|}+{\left|\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0ptD_{t}(2v\cdot w)\right|}.

(9.71)

Let us first estimate
\|D_{t}w_{o}\|_{0}
. Recall from (
2
) that
D_{t}(\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i})^{-1}=\mathfrak{D}v(\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i})^{-1}
, which, combined with the fact that
D_{t}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}=0
, yields

\displaystyle D_{t}w_{o}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k\neq 0}D_{t}{\left(\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}a_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\right)}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}A_{k}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}

\displaystyle{}+\sum_{i,k\neq 0}\sqrt{\sigma_{i}}a_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\mathfrak{D}v\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}A_{k}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}

\displaystyle{}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k\neq 0}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}b_{i,k}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}+\sum_{i,k\neq 0}\mathfrak{D}v\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}.

First notice that, by using (
9.11
), (
9.39
), and (
9.43
), we obtain that

{\left\|\mathfrak{D}v\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}b_{i,k}\right\|}_{0}\lesssim\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}^{1+\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle|k|^{6}$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}^{1+\alpha}$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$|k|^{6}$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-3.5pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle As for the coefficients

, combining (

) and (

) gives

1$}}{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle Therefore

{\left\|D_{t}w_{o}\right\|}_{0}\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}\ell_{q}^{-4\alpha}.

Observing that

D_{t}w_{c}=\sum_{i,k}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}D_{t}c_{i,k}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}},

which follows from
D_{t}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}=0
seen above, (
9.47
) implies

{\left\|D_{t}w_{c}\right\|}_{0}\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}\ell_{q}^{-1-4\alpha}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}.

Combining with
\|w_{o}\|_{0}+\|w_{c}\|_{0}\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}
and using (
4
)-(
4.13
), we obtain that

\displaystyle{\left|\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}D_{t}(w_{o}\otimes w_{c}+w_{c}\otimes w_{o}+w_{c}\otimes w_{c})\right|}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}w_{o}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|w_{c}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|w_{o}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|D_{t}w_{c}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|D_{t}w_{c}\right\|}_{0}{\left\|w_{c}\right\|}_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\delta_{q+1}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}\ell_{q}^{-4\alpha}=\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}(\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q})\ell_{q}^{-4\alpha}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}(\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{1-10}\alpha\lambda_{q+1}^{4\alpha}=\delta_{q+2}\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1-6\alpha}.

The second term of (
9.71
) is thus estimated. We then similarly decompose the first term in (
9.71
) as

\displaystyle D_{t}{\left[\sum_{i,k\neq 0}\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\nabla\Phiup_{i}^{-1}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}\right]}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k\neq 0}D_{t}\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\nabla\Phiup_{i}^{-1}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}

\displaystyle{}+\sum_{i,k\neq 0}\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{D}v\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\nabla\Phiup_{i}^{-1}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}

\displaystyle{}+\sum_{i,k\neq 0}\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}D_{t}[C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})]\nabla\Phiup_{i}^{-1}e^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}

\displaystyle{}+\sum_{i,k\neq 0}\sigma_{i}\mathfrak{D}\Phiup_{i}^{-1}C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\nabla\Phiup_{i}^{-1}\nabla ve^{i\lambda_{q+1}k\cdot\Phiup_{i}}.

In order to estimate this, we still need to estimate
D_{t}[C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})]
and
D_{t}\sigma_{i}
. To obtain the former, we first use (
2.4
):

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}(C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\!)\right\|}_{N}\leq{}

\displaystyle{\left\|(\mathfrak{D}_{2}C_{k})(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{N}{\left\|D_{t}\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|(\mathfrak{D}_{2}C_{k})(\tilde{S}_{i})\right\|}_{0}{\left\|D_{t}\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{N}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle({\left\|C_{k}\right\|}_{N+1}{\left\|\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{1}^{N}+{\left\|C_{k}\right\|}_{2}{\left\|\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{N}){\left\|D_{t}\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|C_{k}\right\|}_{1}{\left\|D_{t}\tilde{S}_{i}\right\|}_{N},

We then use (
2.11
), (
9.42
), and (
9.45
) to conclude that

{\left\|D_{t}(C_{k}(\tilde{S}_{i})\!)\right\|}_{N}\lesssim|k|^{-6}\tau_{q}^{-1}\ell_{q,i}^{-N}.

Coming to
D_{t}\sigma_{i}
, we claim that

{\left\|D_{t}\sigma_{i}\right\|}_{N}\lesssim\delta_{q+1}\tau_{q}^{-1}\ell_{q,i}^{-N}.

To obtain (
9
), we set

\displaystyle h(t)\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\int\eta_{j}^{2}(x,t)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux

\displaystyle D_{t}\sigma_{i}={}

Step 7:
\mathcal{T}_{p}
and its derivative

(cfr. Step 3 above). Thus, recalling that
\theta+\epsilon<\beta

{\left|\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}_{p}\right|}\lesssim 2\Lambdaup^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\cdot\Lambdaup^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{\theta+\epsilon-\beta}+\Lambdaup\lambda_{q+1}^{2\theta+2\epsilon-2\beta}\lesssim\Lambdaup\lambda_{q+1}^{\theta+\epsilon-\beta}.

Since this is exactly (
9.1
), the proposition is proved.

\Diamondx

Remark 9.1
(name=The fractional dissipation term,label=thm:oss:FracDissTerm).

Note that (
9.1
) is stronger than (
9.21
), since

\Lambdaup\lambda_{q+1}^{\theta+\epsilon-\beta}=\Lambdaup^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{\theta+\epsilon}\lesssim\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{1-6\alpha}.

Indeed, this inequality follows from
\theta+\epsilon-\beta<1-6\alpha-\beta-2b\beta
which, for
\alpha,\epsilon
sufficiently small, follows from (
•
‣
4
) and the fact
\theta<\beta
.

However,
\mathcal{T}_{p}
is only estimated as follows:

|\mathcal{T}_{p}(t)|\lesssim t\Lambdaup\lambda_{q+1}^{\theta+\epsilon-\beta}.

To ensure that this satisfies (
9.20
) for any
q\geq 0
, we would require

0<\theta+\epsilon-\beta<-2b^{2}\beta-3b\alpha\iff 3b\alpha<\beta-\theta-\epsilon-2b^{2}\beta.

Seen as the above right-hand side is, in general, negative, we cannot require it. Thus, in general,
\mathcal{T}_{p}
only satisfies (
9.20
) if the
q
in the statement is sufficiently large, which is why we separated
\mathcal{T}_{p}
from the other Reynolds terms.

However, for
t\lesssim\Lambdaup^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{\beta-\epsilon-\theta}
, we can contrast the growth of
\Lambdaup\lambda_{q+1}^{\theta+\epsilon-\beta}
with the smallness of the time, meaning that
\mathcal{T}_{p}
only satisfies (
9.20
) for a short period of time, or if
q
is sufficiently large.

Remark 9.2
(name=
\mathcal{C}^{0}
estimate on the Reynolds stress,label=thm:oss:C1Reyn).

The requirement (
9.2
) is only used to obtain (
9.21
), meaning we only need it on
\operatorname{supp}S
, since
S=0\implies\mathcal{E}=0
.

\displaystyle{\left\|\overline{v}_{q}\right\|}_{N+1+\alpha}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}^{1+\alpha}\ell_{q}^{-N}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathaccent 23{\overline{R}}_{q}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-N-2\alpha}

\displaystyle{\left\|(\partial_{t}+\overline{v}_{q}\cdot\nabla)\mathaccent 23{\overline{R}}_{q}\right\|}_{N+\alpha}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-N-6\alpha}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}.

and

\mathaccent 23{\overline{R}}_{q}\equiv 0\qquad t\!\in\!\bigcup_{i=0}^{\overline{n}}J_{i}.

Recalling
(??)
and (
8
) observe that

{\left[0,\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}2^{-q}T\right]}\!\subset\![0,t_{\overline{n}}],

provided
a\gg 1
is chosen sufficiently large (e.g. so that
\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 5$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}\tau_{q}<\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}2^{-q}T
). Then, choose a cut-off function
{}_{q}\!\in\!\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{C}([0,\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}2^{-q}T];[0,1])
such that

{}_{q}(t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\,}cl@{}}1&t\leq 2^{-(q+1)}T\\
0&t>\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}2^{-q}T\end{array}\right.

and such that
|{}^{\prime}_{q}(t)|\lesssim 2^{q}
. By choosing
a\gg 1
sufficiently large, we may assume that

{\left|{}^{\prime}_{q}(t)\right|}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}

for all
q
. Then, set

S\coloneq{}_{q}^{2}(\overline{R}_{q}-\delta_{q+2}\operatorname{Id})={}_{q}^{2}S.

Using (
10
), (
10.4
), (
10
)-(
10
), and the easy observation that
\overline{\rho}_{q}\lesssim\overline{\rho}_{q}-\delta_{q+2}
, we see that
S
and
(\overline{v}_{q},\overline{p}_{q},\overline{R}_{q})
satisfy the assumptions of
(??)
on the interval
[0,t_{\overline{n}}]
with parameters
\alpha,\hat{\gamma}>0
. We have that

\sigma={}_{q}^{2}(\overline{\rho}_{q}-\delta_{q+2})={}_{q}^{2}\sigma.

Recalling
(??)
, since
\operatorname{supp}S\!\subseteq\![t_{\underline{n}},t_{\overline{n}}]
where (
8.25
) holds, we can apply
(??)
, thus obtaining a new subsolution
(v_{q+1},p_{q+1},\overline{R}_{q}-S-\mathcal{E}_{q+1})
with

\displaystyle{\left\|v_{q+1}-\overline{v}_{q}\right\|}_{0}+\ell_{q}\lambda_{q+1}{\left\|v_{q+1}-\overline{v}_{q}\right\|}_{H^{-1}}\hskip 14.22636pt

\displaystyle{}+\lambda_{q+1}^{-1-\alpha}{\left\|v_{q+1}-\overline{v}_{q}\right\|}_{1+\alpha}\hskip 14.22636pt

\displaystyle{}+\lambda_{q+1}^{-\theta-\epsilon}{\left\|v_{q+1}-\overline{v}_{q}\right\|}_{\theta+\epsilon}\leq{}

\displaystyle M\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt|v_{q+1}|^{2}-\operatorname{tr}S-\operatorname{tr}\mathcal{E}_{q+1}={}

\displaystyle\mskip-4.0mu\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt|\overline{v}_{q}|^{2}\qquad t\!\in\![0,T],

and such that the estimates (
9.20
) and (
9.21
) hold for
\mathcal{E}_{q+1}
.
Let

R_{q+1}\coloneq\overline{R}_{q}-S-\mathcal{E}_{q+1}.

We claim that
(v_{q+1},p_{q+1},R_{q+1})
is a smooth strong subsolution satisfying
(a_{q+1})
-
(g_{q+1})
. Notice that
(a_{q+1})
is satisfied by construction. Since
(v_{q+1},p_{q+1},R_{q+1})=(v_{q},p_{q},R_{q})
for
t\geq 2^{-q}T
, we may restrict
t
to
[0,2^{-q}T]
in the following, so that in particular (
10
) holds.

Let us now prove
(b_{q+1})
. On the one hand

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathaccent 23{R}_{q+1}\right\|}_{0}={}

\displaystyle{\left\|(1-{}_{q}^{2})\mathaccent 23{\overline{R}}_{q}-\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}_{q+1}\right\|}_{0}

\displaystyle{}\leq{}

\displaystyle(1-{}_{q}^{2})\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-2\alpha}+\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha}\bb{1}_{\{{}_{q}>0\}},

(10.16)

on the other hand

\displaystyle\rho_{q+1}={}

\displaystyle(1-{}_{q}^{2})\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}+{}_{q}^{2}\delta_{q+2}+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}\operatorname{tr}\mathcal{E}_{q+1}

\displaystyle{}\geq{}

\displaystyle(1-{}_{q}^{2})\overline{\rho}_{q}+{}_{q}^{2}\delta_{q+2}-\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha}\bb{1}_{\{{}_{q}>0\}}.

(10.17)

The proof of
(b_{q+1})
thus reduces to assessing whether there exists a suitable
\gamma
such that

(1-{}_{q}^{2})\Lambdaup^{-\hat{\gamma}}\overline{\rho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-2\alpha}+\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha}\bb{1}_{\{{}_{q}>0\}}\leq\Lambdaup^{-\gamma}[(1-{}_{q}^{2})\overline{\rho}_{q}+{}_{q}^{2}\delta_{q+2}-\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha}]^{1+\gamma}.

To this end set

\displaystyle F(s)\coloneq{}

\displaystyle(1-s)\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-2\alpha}+\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha}

\displaystyle G(s)\coloneq{}

\displaystyle(1-s)\overline{\rho}_{q}+s\delta_{q+2}-\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha}

\displaystyle H(s)\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\Lambdaup^{-\gamma}G^{1+\gamma}(s)-F(s),

and observe that (
10
) is equivalent to
H({}_{q}^{2})\geq 0
if
{}_{q}>0
, and follows from this inequality also in case
{}_{q}=0
. In particular, (
10
) follows from:

(i)

H(0)\geq 0
and
H(1)\geq 0
;

(ii)

H^{\prime}(0)\leq 0
and
H^{\prime}(1)\leq 0
.

(iii)

H^{\prime\prime}(s)\geq 0
.

We note next that, since
2b\beta\hat{\gamma}<3\alpha

\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha}\lesssim\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}},

so that we have the estimates

F(0)\lesssim\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-2\alpha},\qquad\qquad G(0)\gtrsim\overline{\rho}_{q}.

It is also clear that
G(s)\leq\overline{\rho}_{q}
.

It is then easy to check that the requirement
H(0)\geq 0
, i.e.
F(0)\leq\Lambdaup^{-\gamma}G^{1+\gamma}(0)
, amounts to
\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-2\alpha}\lesssim\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\gamma}
, i.e.
\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{\hat{\gamma}-\gamma}\ell_{q}^{-2\alpha}\lesssim 1
. Hence, since
\ell_{q}^{-1}\leq\lambda_{q+1}
by (
4.13
) and
\overline{\varrho}_{q}\gtrsim\zeta_{q+1}
by
(d_{q+1})
,
H(0)\geq 0
follows from

\hat{\gamma}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\beta$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\beta$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\alpha$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\beta$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\alpha$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\beta$}}$}}>\gamma,

provided
a\gg 1
is sufficiently large to absorb geometric constants. The relation (
10
) follows from (
10.4
) since
b>1
.

The next requirement,
H(1)\geq 0
, i.e.
\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha}\lesssim\zeta_{q+2}(1-\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha})^{1+\gamma}
, requires
3\alpha>2b\beta\gamma
as found in (
10.4
), since
1-\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha}\geq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}
for
a
sufficiently large.

The following condition,
H^{\prime}(0)\leq 0
, can be rewritten as

-\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-2\alpha}\geq(1+\gamma)(\overline{\varrho}_{q}-\zeta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha})(\delta_{q+2}-\overline{\rho}_{q})\Longleftrightarrow\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-2\alpha}\lesssim(\overline{\varrho}_{q}-\zeta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha})(\overline{\rho}_{q}-\delta_{q+2}).

Noting that
\overline{\rho}_{q}\gtrsim\delta_{q+1}\gg\delta_{q+2}
by
(d_{q+1})
, and therefore
\overline{\rho}_{q}-\delta_{q+2}\geq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\overline{\rho}_{q}
for
a
sufficiently large, the above reduces to

\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{\hat{\gamma}-\gamma}\ell_{q}^{-2\alpha}\lesssim 1\Longleftarrow\lambda_{q+1}^{2\alpha-2\beta\hat{\gamma}+2\beta\gamma}=\zeta_{q+1}^{\hat{\gamma}-\gamma}\lambda_{q+1}^{2\alpha}\lesssim 1,

which follows from condition (
10
) deduced above.

We then need the condition
H^{\prime}(1)\leq 0
, which can be rewritten as

-\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-2\alpha}\geq(1+\gamma)\zeta_{q+2}(1-\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha})(\delta_{q+2}-\overline{\rho}_{q}),

which similarly follows from (
10
).

The last condition,
H^{\prime\prime}\geq 0
, follows from the fact that
F^{\prime\prime}\equiv 0
and
G^{\prime\prime}\equiv 0
, and thus
H^{\prime\prime}=\Lambdaup^{-\gamma}(1+\gamma)\gamma G^{\gamma-1}G^{\prime 2}
is positive.

Thus, our choice of
\alpha,\gamma,\hat{\gamma}
in (
10.4
) guarantees that (
10
) holds, which yields
(b_{q+1})
.

Consider now
(c_{q+1})
, where we only need to consider the case
j=q+1
, i.e. the estimate on
[2^{-q-1}T,2^{-q}T]
. Using (
10.17
), the fact that
\overline{\rho}_{q}\geq\delta_{q+2}
for
a
large enough, and (
10
), we see that

\delta_{q+2}(1-\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha})\leq\rho_{q+1}(t)\leq\overline{\rho}_{q}(t)+\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta_{q+1}+\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha}.

Therefore
(c_{q+1})
holds, provided
a\gg 1
is sufficiently large.

Similarly, concerning
(d_{q+1})
, observe that for
t\leq 2^{-(q+1)}T
we have that
{}_{q}(t)=1
, so that

\delta_{q+2}(1-\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha})\leq\rho_{q+1}\leq\delta_{q+2}(1+\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha}).

Moreover, using (
10.16
) and the fact that
{}_{q}=1
for
t\leq 2^{-(q+1)}T

{\left\|\mathaccent 23{R}_{q+1}\right\|}_{0}\leq\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-3\alpha}\leq\Lambdaup{\left(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}\zeta_{q+2}\right)}^{1+\hat{\gamma}},

where we used the fact that
2b\beta\hat{\gamma}<3\alpha
and chose
a\gg 1
sufficiently large. Therefore
(d_{q+1})
, i.e.
\|\mathaccent 23{R}_{q+1}\|_{0}\lesssim\Lambdaup\varrho_{q+1}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}
and
\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}\delta_{q+2}\leq\rho_{q+1}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 5$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}\delta_{q+2}
, holds.

Concerning
(e_{q+1})
, it is once more enough to restrict to
t\leq 2^{-q}T
, i.e. the case
j=q+1
. From (
10
) and (
9.18
) we deduce that

\displaystyle{\left\|v_{q+1}\right\|}_{1+\alpha}\leq{}

\displaystyle{\left\|v_{q+1}-\overline{v}_{q}\right\|}_{1+\alpha}+{\left\|\overline{v}_{q}\right\|}_{1+\alpha}

\displaystyle{}\leq{}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle M$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle M$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$M$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$M$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+\alpha}+C\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}^{1+\alpha}

\displaystyle{}\leq{}

\displaystyle M\delta_{q+1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{1+\alpha},

where
C
is the implicit constant in (
10
), which can be absorbed by choosing
a\gg 1
sufficiently large. The estimate on
|\partial_{t}\rho_{q+1}|
similarly follows from the trace estimate of (
10
) and (
9.21
).
(e_{q+1})
is thus proved.

(f_{q+1})
follows from (
10
), (
9.17
), and (
9.16
).

Finally,
(g_{q+1})
easily follows from (
9.19
) and (
10
).

Step 4: Convergence to an adapted subsolution

We have thus obtained a sequence
(v_{q},p_{q},R_{q})
satisfying
(a_{q})
-
(g_{q})
.

From
(f_{q})
it follows that
(v_{q},p_{q})
is a Cauchy sequence in
\mathcal{C}^{0}
. Indeed, it is clear for
\{v_{q}\}
, and concerning
\{p_{q}\}
we may use (
3.1
) to write

\Deltaup(p_{q+1}-p_{q})=-\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}(\mathaccent 23{R}_{q+1}-\mathaccent 23{R}_{q}+(v_{q+1}-v_{q})\otimes v_{q}+v_{q+1}\otimes(v_{q+1}-v_{q})\!),

and apply Schauder estimates (
(??)
). Similarly,
\{R_{q}\}
also converges in
\mathcal{C}^{0}
. Indeed, from the definition and using (
8.20
), (
8.12
), (
9.10
), (
9.20
), and
(b_{q})
, we have that

\displaystyle{\left\|R_{q+1}-R_{q}\right\|}_{0}={}

\displaystyle{\left\|\overline{R}_{q}-R_{q}-S-\mathcal{E}_{q+1}\right\|}_{0}

\displaystyle{}\leq{}

\displaystyle{\left\|\overline{R}_{q}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|R_{q}\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|S\right\|}_{0}+{\left\|\mathcal{E}_{q+1}\right\|}_{0}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\delta_{q+1}.

For all
t>0
there exists
q(t)\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
such that

(v_{q},p_{q},R_{q})(\cdot,t)=(v_{q(t)},p_{q(t)},R_{q(t)})(\cdot,t)\qquad\forall q\geq q(t),

thus
(v_{q},p_{q},R_{q})
converges uniformly to a strong subsolution
(\hat{v},\hat{p},\hat{R})
satisfying

{\left\|\hat{R}\right\|}_{0}\leq\Lambdaup\hat{\varrho}^{1+\gamma},

and, using (
7.11
) and
(a_{q})

\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big(|\hat{v}|^{2}+\operatorname{tr}\hat{R}\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux=\mskip-4.0mu\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big(|\tilde{v}|^{2}+\operatorname{tr}\tilde{R}\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\qquad\forall t\!\in\![0,T].

Furthermore, using (
7.14
) and
(f_{q})

\displaystyle{\left\|\hat{v}-\tilde{v}\right\|}_{H^{-1}}\leq{}

\displaystyle{\left\|v_{0}-\tilde{v}\right\|}_{H^{-1}}+{\left\|v_{0}-\hat{v}\right\|}_{H^{-1}}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\delta_{1}\lambda_{0}^{-1}+\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}{\left\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\right\|}_{H^{-1}}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle\delta^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\zeta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\gamma$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\gamma$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\gamma$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\gamma$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\ell_{q}^{\alpha},

leading to (
5.3
) for
a
sufficiently large. Using
(f_{q})
and the fact that
\tilde{v},\check{v}
are smooth and thus bounded in
\mathcal{C}^{0}
, (
5.2
) is proved similarly:

\displaystyle{\left\|\hat{v}-\tilde{v}\right\|}_{\mathcal{C}^{0}}\leq{}

\displaystyle{\left\|v_{0}-\tilde{v}\right\|}_{\mathcal{C}^{0}}+{\left\|v_{0}-\hat{v}\right\|}_{\mathcal{C}^{0}}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{q=0}^{\infty}{\left\|v_{q+1}-v_{q}\right\|}_{0}

\displaystyle{}\lesssim{}

\displaystyle 1+\delta_{1}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}.

Concerning the initial datum, from
(e_{q})
and
(f_{q})
we obtain by interpolation that
\hat{v}(\cdot,0)\!\in\!\mathcal{C}^{\hat{\beta}}
, and from
(d_{q})
we obtain that
\hat{R}(\cdot,0)=0
.

Finally, we verify conditions (
3.5
), and (
3.6
) for being a
\mathcal{C}^{\hat{\beta}}
-adapted subsolution. Let
t>0
. Then there exists
q\!\in\!\mathbb{N}
such that
t\!\in\![2^{-q}T,2^{-q+1}T]
. By
(c_{q})
and
(e_{q})

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 8$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 8$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$8$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$8$}}$}}\delta_{q+1}\leq{}

\displaystyle\hat{\rho}\leq 4\delta_{q}

\displaystyle{\left\|\hat{v}\right\|}_{1+\alpha}\leq{}

\displaystyle M\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}^{1+\alpha}.

Therefore
\hat{\rho}^{-1}\geq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}\delta_{q}^{-1}
, and hence, using (
4
) and (
10.3
), we deduce that

\displaystyle{\left\|\hat{v}\right\|}_{1+\alpha}\leq{}

\displaystyle\Lambdaup^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\hat{\varrho}^{-(1+\nu)},

for
a\gg 1
sufficiently large. Similarly, using
(e_{q})
and (
10.3
), we deduce that

{\left|\partial_{t}\hat{\rho}\right|}\lesssim\delta_{q+1}\delta_{q}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}=\Lambdaup^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}^{1-\beta}\lambda_{q+1}^{-2\beta}\sim\Lambdaup^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{q}^{1-\beta-2b\beta}=\Lambdaup^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\zeta_{q}^{-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2\beta$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2\beta$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2\beta$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2\beta$}}$}}(1-\beta-2b\beta)}\leq\Lambdaup^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\zeta_{q}^{1-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1-\beta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2\beta$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1-\beta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2\beta$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1-\beta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2\beta$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1-\beta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2\beta$}}$}}}\lesssim\Lambdaup^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\hat{\varrho}^{-\nu}.

Finally, a word about the term

\hat{\mathcal{T}}\coloneq\sum(\mathcal{T}_{g}^{(q)}+\mathcal{T}_{d}^{(q)}),

where
\mathcal{T}_{g}^{(q)}
and
\mathcal{T}_{d}^{(q)}
are the extra trace terms from the
q
th gluing and perturbation steps. We have that
|\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}_{g}^{(q)}|+|\partial_{t}\mathcal{T}_{d}^{(q)}|\lesssim\Lambdaup\lambda_{q}^{\theta+\epsilon-\beta}
, thus proving (
5.1
). However, adding
\hat{\mathcal{T}}
into
\hat{R}
could compromise the adaptedness of
(\hat{v},\hat{p},\hat{R})
by rendering (
3.5
)-(
3.6
) invalid, which is why we keep it separated and deal with it in the final argument. The estimate (
5.1
) implies that

{\left|\hat{\mathcal{T}}(t)\right|}\lesssim\sum t\Lambdaup\lambda_{q}^{\theta+\epsilon-\beta}.

To be able to make it as small as we desire, we must contrast the
a
-growth of the
q=0
and
q=1
terms of this sum. This is easily achieved by requiring
t\leq\Lambdaup^{-1}\lambda_{0}^{\beta-\theta-\epsilon-\iota}
for
\iota
arbitrarily small. In any case, calling
t_{s}
the maximal time where
\hat{\mathcal{T}}
can be estimated with small quantities, we have that

\lim_{a\to\infty}t_{s}=0,

since we meed
t_{s}\delta_{0}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{0}^{\theta+\epsilon}
to be small.

provided

is sufficiently large. These two relations, combined with (

), yield (

).

Start from
(v_{0},p_{0},R_{0})
, we will inductively construct a sequence
(v_{q},p_{q},R_{q})
of smooth strong subsolutions for

, with

satisfying the following properties:

(A_{q})

For all
t\!\in\![0,T]

\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big(|v_{q}|^{2}+\operatorname{tr}R_{q}\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux=\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\big(|v_{0}|^{2}+\operatorname{tr}R_{0}\big)}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux;

(B_{q})
