\displaystyle\overline{\rho}_{q}(1-{}_{q}^{2})+{}_{q}^{2}\delta_{q+2}-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}\operatorname{tr}\mathcal{E}_{q+1}

\displaystyle\mathaccent 23{R}_{q+1}={}

\displaystyle\mathaccent 23{\overline{R}}_{q}(1-{}_{q}^{2})-\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}_{q+1}.

For
t\!\in\!K_{q}
, condition
(B_{q+1})
follows easily from (
9.20
) and the fact that
K_{q}\!\subset\!\{{}_{q}=1\}
. For
t\!\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.33334pt\kern-4.99998pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\in}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.33334pt\kern-4.99998pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\in}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.69444pt\kern-4.18054pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\in}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.36115pt\kern-3.68057pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\in}}}\!J_{q}
, we have that

\rho_{q+1}=\rho_{q}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta_{q+2}<\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 5$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta_{q+2}.

For
t\!\in\!J_{q}\smallsetminus K_{q}
, we have that
\overline{\rho}_{q}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 9$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 8$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 9$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 8$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$9$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$8$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$9$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$8$}}$}}\rho_{q}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 9$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 8$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 9$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 8$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$9$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$8$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$9$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$8$}}$}}\cdot 2\delta_{q+2}=\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 9$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 9$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$9$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$9$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}\delta_{q+2}
, which means

\rho_{q+1}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 9$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 9$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$9$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$9$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}\delta_{q+2}{\left(1-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 9$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 9$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$4$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$9$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$4$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$9$}}$}}{}_{q}^{2}+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 9$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 5$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 9$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$9$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$9$}}$}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-6\alpha}\right)}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 4$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 5$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 4$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$4$}}$}}\delta_{q+2}{\left(\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 9$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 5$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 9$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 5$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$9$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$5$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$9$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$5$}}$}}+\lambda_{1}^{-6\alpha}\right)},

and if
\lambda_{1}^{-6\alpha}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 5$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 5$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$5$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$5$}}$}}
, which is a matter of choosing
a
large enough, we have
(B_{q+1})
.

Note that, by the construction of
\rho_{q+1}
, we have that
J_{q}\!\subseteq\!K_{q+1}
, since on the whole of
J_{q}
we have that
\rho_{q+1}\sim\delta_{q+2}\gg\delta_{q+3}
. This is the reason why we required
\hat{\gamma}<\gamma
and used the larger

outside of

in

: to make sure
(C_{q+1})
was automatically verified outside
J_{q}
. This is in stark contrast to what happened in Section
10
, where the perturbation regions
P_{q}\coloneq[0,2^{-q}T]
satisfied the opposite inclusion
P_{q+1}\!\subseteq\!P_{q}
, and where we consequently required
\hat{\gamma}>\gamma
to ensure the weaker “strong condition”

in
P_{q}\smallsetminus P_{q+1}
, while the stronger
(d_{q+1})
only held in
P_{q+1}
, where
{}_{q}=1
.

By the above paragraph, in verifying conditions
(C_{q+1})
-
(D_{q+1})
, it suffices to restrict to the case when
\rho_{q+1}\geq 2\delta_{q+3}
and
j=q+1
, respectively.

The argument showing
(C_{q+1})
for
t\!\in\!K_{q+1}
is similar to the proof of
(b_{q+1})
in Step 3 of
(??)
above. On the one hand

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathaccent 23{R}_{q+1}\right\|}_{0}={}

\displaystyle{\left\|(1-{}_{q}^{2})\mathaccent 23{\overline{R}}_{q}-\mathaccent 23{\mathcal{E}}_{q+1}\right\|}_{0}

\displaystyle{}\leq{}

\displaystyle(1-{}_{q}^{2})\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-2\alpha+(1-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle b$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle b$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}})\alpha\bb{1}_{{}_{q}=1}}+\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-6\alpha},

on the other hand

\displaystyle\rho_{q+1}={}

\displaystyle(1-{}_{q}^{2})\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}+{}_{q}^{2}\delta_{q+2}+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}\operatorname{tr}\mathcal{E}_{q+1}

\displaystyle{}\geq{}

\displaystyle(1-{}_{q}^{2})\overline{\rho}_{q}+{}_{q}^{2}\delta_{q+2}-\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-6\alpha}.

So where

we have the condition since, for

since

To this end set

\displaystyle F(s)\coloneq{}

\displaystyle(1-s)\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\tilde{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-2\alpha}+\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-6\alpha}

\displaystyle G(s)\coloneq{}

\displaystyle(1-s)\overline{\rho}_{q}+s\delta_{q+2}-\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-6\alpha}=\overline{\rho}_{q}+s(\delta_{q+2}-\overline{\rho}_{q})-\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-6\alpha}

and, just like in

, deduce that

by proving that:

and

To this end, we first obtain the estimates

\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-6\alpha}\lesssim\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}},\qquad\quad F(0)\lesssim\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-2\alpha},\qquad G(0)\gtrsim\overline{\rho}_{q},\qquad\quad G(s)\leq\overline{\rho}_{q}.

The first one follows from (

), (

), and the fact we are working for

.. The second one follows from the first one. The fourth one is obvious, since
\overline{\rho}_{q}\geq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 7$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 8$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 7$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 8$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$7$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$8$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$7$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$8$}}$}}\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta_{q+2}\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 21$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 16$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 21$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 16$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$21$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$16$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$21$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$16$}}$}}\delta_{q+2}>\delta_{q+2}
. For the third one, we reduce it to
\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-6\alpha}\lesssim\overline{\rho}_{q}
, and then it follows from the first estimate, since
\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\leq\overline{\rho}_{q}
. We then prove (i)-(v) as follows.

•

It is easy to check that the two parts of (i) amount to

\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-2\alpha}\lesssim\Lambdaup\overline{\varrho}_{q}^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q+1}^{-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle b$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle b$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}}\alpha},\qquad\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-6\alpha}\leq\Lambdaup^{-\hat{\gamma}}[\delta_{q+2}(1-\lambda_{q+1}^{-6\alpha})]^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q+1}^{-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle b$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle b$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}}\alpha};

The requirements (ii) can be rewritten as

Step 3: From one to infinitely many

Looking at the details of the scheme, we realize that replacing
(\hat{v},\hat{p},\hat{R})
with

(v_{0},p_{0},R_{0})\coloneq(\hat{v},\hat{p},\hat{R}+{}^{e}/_{3}),

as described in the statement of
(??)
, clearly retains condition (
3.3
), since the initial datum is not changed. It does not necessarily preserve conditions (
3.5
), and (

). Those, however, are only needed to obtain the conditions
(D_{0})
. If we then show that the conditions
(A_{0})-(F_{0})
(and thus also

) are maintained with such a perturbation, we need not worry about losing (
3.5
) and (

).

Conditions about the velocity are clearly preserved, and
(A_{0})
and
(E_{0})
are vacuous, so all we need is

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{\prime}\leq{}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 5$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$5$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta

(
B_{0}
)

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathaccent 23{R}_{0}^{\prime}\right\|}_{0}\leq{}

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\,}cl@{}}\Lambdaup\varrho_{0}^{\prime 1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle b$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle b$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}}\alpha}&\rho_{0}^{\prime}\geq 2\delta_{2}\\
\Lambdaup\varrho_{0}^{\prime 1+\hat{\gamma}}&\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta_{2}\leq\rho_{0}^{\prime}\leq 2\delta_{2}\\
\Lambdaup\varrho_{0}^{\prime 1+\gamma}&\rho_{0}^{\prime}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta_{2}\end{array}\right.

(
C_{0}
)

\displaystyle{\left|\partial_{t}\rho_{0}^{\prime}\right|}\leq{}

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{\prime}\delta_{0}^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\lambda_{0}.

(
D_{0}
.2, i.e. (
11.10
))

Concerning

, the proposition assumes
\hat{\rho}\leq{}^{{}_{{}^{\scriptstyle 5}}}\hskip-2.0pt/{\hskip-2.0pt\,}_{2}\delta
, so that the condition is preserved by requiring (

). Since

,

has the possibility to vary in a neighborhood of

without becoming negative.

(D_{0}.3)
boils down to the following condition on

:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\,}cl@{}}{\left|\partial_{t}e\right|}\leq e\sqrt{\delta_{0}}\lambda_{0}&e>0\\
{\left|\partial_{t}e\right|}+{\left|e\right|}\sqrt{\delta_{0}}\lambda_{0}\leq\hat{\rho}\sqrt{\delta_{0}}\lambda_{0}-{\left|\partial_{t}\hat{\rho}\right|}&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right..

To keep things simple, we require (
5.14
) and (
5.15
).

Coming to
(C_{0})
, we first assume
e>0
, which immediately yields, by the properties of
(\hat{v},\hat{p},\hat{R})
, that

{\left\|\mathaccent 23{R}_{0}\right\|}_{0}\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\,}cl@{}}\Lambdaup(\hat{\varrho}+\Lambdaup^{-1}e)^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle b$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle b$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}}\alpha}&\hat{\rho}\geq 2\delta_{2}\\
\Lambdaup(\hat{\varrho}+\Lambdaup^{-1}e)^{1+\hat{\gamma}}&\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta_{2}\leq\hat{\rho}\leq 2\delta_{2}\\
\Lambdaup(\hat{\varrho}+\Lambdaup^{-1}e)^{1+\gamma}&\hat{\rho}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta_{2}\end{array}\right..

Our goal is to obtain that

{\left\|\mathaccent 23{R}_{0}\right\|}_{0}\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{\,}cl@{}}\Lambdaup(\hat{\varrho}+\Lambdaup^{-1}e)^{1+\hat{\gamma}}\ell_{q}^{-\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle b$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle b$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$b$}}$}}\alpha}&\hat{\rho}+e\geq 2\delta_{2}\\
\Lambdaup(\hat{\varrho}+\Lambdaup^{-1}e)^{1+\hat{\gamma}}&\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta_{2}\leq\hat{\rho}+e\leq 2\delta_{2}\\
\Lambdaup(\hat{\varrho}+\Lambdaup^{-1}e)^{1+\gamma}&\hat{\rho}+e\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$3$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\delta_{2}\end{array}\right..

•

We first note that
\hat{\rho}+e\leq{}^{3}/_{2}\delta_{2}\implies\hat{\rho}\leq{}^{3}/_{2}\delta_{2}
, so in this case we have the desired estimate for
\|\mathaccent 23{R}_{0}\|_{0}
;

•

If
\hat{\rho}\leq{}^{3}/_{2}\delta_{2}
but
{}^{3}/_{2}\delta_{2}\leq\hat{\rho}+e\leq 2\delta_{2}
, since
\hat{\gamma}<\gamma
and
\|\mathaccent 23{R}_{0}\|_{0}\leq\Lambdaup(\hat{\varrho}+\Lambdaup^{-1}e)^{1+\gamma}
, we have the desired estimate;

•

If
\hat{\rho}\leq{}^{3}/_{2}\delta_{2}
but
\hat{\rho}+e\geq 2\delta_{2}
, the desired estimate is even looser than in the previous item;

•

If
{}^{3}/_{2}\delta_{2}\leq\hat{\rho}\leq 2\delta_{2}
, then either
\hat{\rho}+e
also satisfies this bound, in which case we have the desired estimate, or
\hat{\rho}+e\geq 2\delta_{2}
, in which case the desired estimate is looser;

•

Finally, if
\hat{\rho}\geq 2\delta_{2}
, then so is
\hat{\rho}+e
, meaning again we have the desired estimate.

Thus, we need no additional conditions to obtain
(C_{0})
. Summing up, the conditions we must impose on
e
are precisely (
5.13
)-(
5.15
). The proof is complete.

Proof.

Fix
\rho\!\in\!\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(B_{1}(0)\!)
a standard mollification kernel in space, and define:

\rho_{\epsilon}(x)\coloneq\epsilon^{-3}\rho(x\epsilon^{-1}).

To ensure the regularity of the initial datum, we consider the smoothed datum

w_{0}\coloneq w\ast\rho_{\eta_{0}},

where

\eta_{0}\coloneq\max{\left\{\eta:\|w\ast\rho_{\eta}-w\|_{L^{2}}\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}\wedge\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}[{\left|w_{0}\right|}^{2}-{\left|w\right|}^{2}](x)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$2$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}\delta\right\}}.

By
(??)
, there exists a solution
(\tilde{v},\tilde{p})
with initial datum
w_{0}
, where
\tilde{p}
can be recovered uniquely once we impose
\int\tilde{p}=0
.

We now fix a standard mollification kernel in time
\chi\!\in\!\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\!(-1,0)\!)
and, with
\rho_{\epsilon},\rho
as defined above, we define

\displaystyle\chi_{\epsilon}(t)\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\epsilon^{-1}\chi(t\epsilon^{-1})

\displaystyle v(x,t)\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\int\limits_{t\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut+\epsilon}\hskip-2.0pt(\tilde{v}\ast\rho_{\epsilon})(x,s)\chi_{\epsilon}(t-s)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus,

\displaystyle p(x,t)\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\int\limits_{t\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut+\epsilon}\hskip-2.0pt(\tilde{p}\ast\rho_{\epsilon})(x,s)\chi_{\epsilon}(t-s)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus,

\displaystyle R(x,t)\coloneq{}

\displaystyle\overline{\tilde{v}\otimes\tilde{v}}-v\otimes v,

where

\overline{f}=\int_{t}^{t+\epsilon}(f\ast\rho_{\epsilon})(x,s)\chi_{\epsilon}(t-s)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus.

By construction and since

By using Jensen’s inequality on

with the measure
\rho_{\epsilon}(x-y)\chi_{\epsilon}(t-s)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus

R=\overline{\tilde{v}\otimes\tilde{v}}-v\otimes v\geq 0.

Coming to the initial datum, we have that

Taking the

{\left\|v|_{t=0}-w_{0}\right\|}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}\leq\sup_{\mathclap{t\in[0,\epsilon]}}{\left\|\tilde{v}(\cdot,t)-w_{0}\right\|}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}+{\left\|w_{0}\ast\rho_{\epsilon}-w_{0}\right\|}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}\eqcolon\sup_{\mathclap{t\in[0,\epsilon]}}I_{t}+II_{\epsilon}.

6.2

{}+\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}[(\chi_{\epsilon}\ast{\left|\tilde{v}\right|}^{2})-{\left|\tilde{v}\right|}^{2}](x,t)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux+\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left[{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}v\right|}^{2}-{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}\right|}^{2}\right]}(x,s)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus\\
{\hskip-56.9055pt}\eqcolon\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$1$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|w\right|}^{2}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux+I+II+III.

Our desired estimate (
6.3
) will then follow from

I\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}\qquad II\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}\qquad III\leq\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 3$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\delta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 3$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\delta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$3$}}$}}.

The first of these relations follows from (
A
).

The second relation in (
A
) follows, for
\epsilon
sufficiently small, from the fact that, since
\tilde{v}\!\in\!L^{2}_{t}H^{\theta}_{x}\!\subseteq\!L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}
,
|\tilde{v}|^{2}\!\in\!L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}
, so
\chi_{\epsilon}\ast|\tilde{v}|^{2}\to|\tilde{v}|^{2}
in
L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}
for
\epsilon\to 0
.

Coming to the third relation, we first rewrite the integral of the first integrand:

\displaystyle\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\overline{v}\right|}^{2}(x,s)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus={}

\displaystyle\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\left|\int\limits_{s\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mus+\epsilon}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt\rho_{\epsilon}(x-y)(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}(y,\tau)\chi_{\epsilon}(t-\tau)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5muy\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mu\tau\right|}^{2}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus

\displaystyle{}\leq{}

\displaystyle\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{s\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mus+\epsilon}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}\hskip-2.0pt{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}\right|}^{2}(x,\tau)\chi_{\epsilon}(t-\tau)\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mu\tau\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus,

where we used Jensen’s inequality in the first step and (
A
) in the second one. Therefore, the remaining term is rewritten as:

III=\int\limits_{0\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mut}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}^{3}\mskip 4.0mu}^{\mskip 4.0mu}{\left[{\left(\chi_{\epsilon}\ast{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}\right|}^{2}\right)}(x,s)-{\left|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}\right|}^{2}(x,s)\right]}\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mux\mskip 0.0mu\mathrm{d}\mskip 0.5mus.

We now note that, since
\tilde{v}\!\in\!L^{2}_{t}H^{\theta}_{x}
, we have that
(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}\!\in\!L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}
, and thus
|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}|^{2}\!\in\!L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}
. Therefore, as before,
\chi_{\epsilon}\ast|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}|^{2}-|(-\Deltaup)^{\mathchoice{\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle 2$}}}{\frac{\raisebox{1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}{\raisebox{-1.5pt}{$\scriptstyle 2$}}}{\scalebox{0.5}[0.5]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}{\scalebox{0.4}[0.4]{$\dfrac{\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\theta$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$2$}}$}}}\tilde{v}|^{2}\to 0
in
L^{1}_{t}L^{1}_{x}
, and the third relation of (
A
) reduces to an opportune choice of
\epsilon
.

Summing up,
(v,p,R)
is a smooth solution of (
3.1
), which satisfies (
6.3
) and (
6.2
), and
R\geq 0
by (
A
). The proof of
(??)
is thus complete.
